14:T46b,**Describe the bug**
In `packages/core/src/utils/code-fence.ts`, `unwrapWholeCodeFence` strictly checks `value.startsWith("")`:
```ts
export function unwrapWholeCodeFence(
	value: string,
	languages: readonly string[],
): string | null {
	if (!value.startsWith("") || value.length < 6) {
		return null;
	}
```
LLM completions often produce code fences with leading or trailing newlines or whitespace (e.g., `"\n```json\n{...}\n```\n"`). Because `unwrapWholeCodeFence` does not trim the outer input before checking delimiters, valid code blocks are rejected with `null`.

**To Reproduce**
1. Call `unwrapWholeCodeFence("\n```json\n{\"ok\": true}\n```\n", ["json"])`
2. Observe return value is `null` instead of `"{\"ok\": true}"`.

**Expected behavior**
- `unwrapWholeCodeFence` should trim outer whitespace from the input string before parsing code fence delimiters.

**Evidence / reproduction proof**
- [x] Unit test in `packages/core/src/utils/code-fence.test.ts`
- [x] Backend logs: N/A - pure parser utility
- [x] Real-LLM trajectory: N/A - code fence unwrap helper
- [x] Domain artifacts: N/A - no persistent storage modified
15:T41b,**Describe the bug**
In `plugins/plugin-documents/src/document-presenter.ts`, `truncateLabel` checks `value.charCodeAt(end - 1)`:
```ts
function truncateLabel(value: string, maxLength = 80): string {
  if (value.length <= maxLength) return value;
  let end = maxLength - 1;
  const lastKept = value.charCodeAt(end - 1);
  if (lastKept >= 0xd800 && lastKept <= 0xdbff) end -= 1;
  return `${value.slice(0, end).trimEnd()}...`;
}
```
If `maxLength <= 1`, `end = maxLength - 1` is `0` or negative, causing `end - 1` to be negative (`-1`), resulting in an out-of-bounds `charCodeAt(-1)` check producing `NaN`.

**To Reproduce**
1. Inspect `truncateLabel` with small maxLength values.

**Expected behavior**
- Guard `end > 0` before inspecting `value.charCodeAt(end - 1)`.

**Evidence / reproduction proof**
- [x] Unit test in `plugins/plugin-documents/src/document-presenter.test.ts`
- [x] Backend logs: N/A - pure document presenter helper
- [x] Real-LLM trajectory: N/A - document formatting
- [x] Domain artifacts: N/A - no persistent storage modified

18:T4a5,**Describe the bug**
In `packages/core/src/utils/fs-extra-lite.ts`, `pathExists` catches `fsp.access` rejections to return boolean `false`:
```ts
export async function pathExists(target: string): Promise<boolean> {
	try {
		await fsp.access(target);
		return true;
	} catch {
		return false;
	}
}
```
Per the repository error policy in `AGENTS.md`:
"Every retained catch must document one of these grep-able categories on the handler with `// error-policy:J<N> <reason>`:
- J4 — user-facing degrade: only an expected error shape becomes a visibly distinct unavailable/error state."

This catch block is missing the mandatory `// error-policy:J4` comment.

**To Reproduce**
1. Inspect `packages/core/src/utils/fs-extra-lite.ts:L23`
2. Notice bare catch block without `// error-policy:J<N>`.

**Expected behavior**
- Catch block should document `// error-policy:J4` explaining the translation of access rejections to `false`.

**Evidence / reproduction proof**
- [x] Unit test in `packages/core/src/utils/fs-extra-lite.test.ts`

1a:T450,**Describe the bug**
In `packages/core/src/utils/node.ts`, `getLocalServerUrl(path: string)` normalizes paths using:

```ts
const normalizedPath = path && !path.startsWith("/") ? `/${path}` : path;
```

When `path` is `undefined` or omitted (e.g. `getLocalServerUrl()` or `getLocalServerUrl(undefined)`), `normalizedPath` is `undefined`, causing string interpolation to produce `"http://localhost:3000undefined"`.

1b:T895,Parent account-readiness epic: #25025
Parent domain-consolidation contract: #18806
Related cross-origin auth owner: #19214
Related launch-login UX owner: #28581

## Problem

The public landing page conditionally renders **Sign in** or **Dashboard**, but the condition is not connected to the canonical Steward session:

- `packages/homepage/src/pages/landing.tsx` reads the legacy key `localStorage.eliza_app_session`.
- The canonical Steward client stores `steward_session_token`.
- The public landing and Cloud app use different origins (`staging.eliza.app` and `cloud-staging.eliza.app`), so origin-scoped storage cannot make the landing observe the Cloud session directly.

As a result, the Dashboard branch is effectively unreachable for a normal current login, or may be activated by stale legacy state rather than current authenticated state. The front door cannot truthfully communicate whether the user is signed in.

This was found through a read-only audit of the live Pages artifact and byte-identical `origin/develop` sources. No account, credential, token, OAuth grant or browser storage was read or changed.

## Expected contract

Make an explicit product decision:

1. the public landing always uses a truthful neutral/sign-in CTA; or
2. it reflects Cloud authentication through a safe, short-lived cross-origin signal designed under #18806.

Do not copy the Steward bearer token into the marketing origin and do not introduce a broad `.eliza.app` cookie.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Remove the legacy `eliza_app_session` heuristic or replace it with the approved safe session-presence contract.
- [ ] A current Steward session created on `cloud-staging.eliza.app` produces the intended landing CTA after returning to `staging.eliza.app`.
- [ ] Logout, revocation, expiration and stale legacy storage cannot leave a false signed-in Dashboard state.
- [ ] Staging and production remain isolated.
- [ ] No bearer token is exposed to the public marketing origin.
- [ ] Automated browser coverage exercises signed out, signed in, expired/revoked and stale-storage states across both origins.
- [ ] CTA wording and destination remain coherent with the launch handoff owned by #28581.1c:T12b4,## Problem

#28408 broadened `keepAliveAfterComplete` to ordinary durable Smithers sessions so they can accept follow-ups. The retained ACP session becomes `ready`, while its durable owner task remains terminal. Two existing lifecycle consumers interpret that split state unsafely:

1. **The watchdog treats intentional promptable idle as stalled work.** `detectStalledSessions` excludes only terminal ACP statuses, so a retained `ready` session crosses the default three-minute threshold and receives `STALL_GRILL_PROMPT`. That creates an unsolicited model turn after completed work; because activity refreshes and the dedupe set can clear, the cycle can repeat.
2. **Capacity reclamation can stop an in-flight follow-up.** `reclaimIdleSession` also excludes only terminal ACP statuses. A follow-up changes the ACP session to `busy`, but its durable owner task is still terminal, so queue pressure can select and stop that busy session.

The merge commit [`0cc958b7`](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/commit/0cc958b7ca9cb8ecbd377c99fd3bcdd02d976ee4) is an ancestor of current `develop` [`e68ba2e8`](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/commit/e68ba2e84ee09a0b5cb6c6a4b7e03861be295ba0). The relevant production blobs are byte-identical between them.

## Current code path

- Durable Smithers retention is enabled in [`TASKS:create`](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/e68ba2e84ee09a0b5cb6c6a4b7e03861be295ba0/plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/actions/tasks.ts#L909-L919).
- A completed retained prompt returns to [`ready`](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/e68ba2e84ee09a0b5cb6c6a4b7e03861be295ba0/plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/services/acp-service.ts#L3755-L3759).
- The watchdog filters only terminal states, then sends the grill prompt to every stale candidate ([detector](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/e68ba2e84ee09a0b5cb6c6a4b7e03861be295ba0/plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/services/task-watchdog-service.ts#L62-L77), [dispatch](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/e68ba2e84ee09a0b5cb6c6a4b7e03861be295ba0/plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/services/task-watchdog-service.ts#L272-L300)).
- A follow-up becomes [`busy`](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/e68ba2e84ee09a0b5cb6c6a4b7e03861be295ba0/plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/services/acp-service.ts#L2498-L2516), but the reclaimer still accepts every nonterminal ACP state and stops the selected session ([reclaimer](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/e68ba2e84ee09a0b5cb6c6a4b7e03861be295ba0/plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/services/orchestrator-task-service.ts#L7274-L7313)).

## Reproduction evidence

I added two disposable desired-behavior probes at the exact merged PR head and ran them with the existing admission suite:

```text
Test Files  2 failed (2)
Tests       2 failed | 11 passed (13)

retained ready watchdog probe
Expected: []
Received: [{ "id": "retained-ready", "idleMs": 180000 }]

cap=1 busy follow-up admission probe
Expected session status: "busy"
Received session status: "stopped"
```

The first probe passes one `ready` session idle for exactly `180_000` ms to `detectStalledSessions` and expects no stalled work. The second uses the existing admission integration harness: spawn task A at cap 1, mark A terminal, mark its retained session `busy` to model a follow-up, queue task B, and assert A remains busy. Both assertions fail against the merged behavior.

The later live-smoke change in #28387 covers a happy-path sequential follow-up, but it does not wait through the watchdog threshold or create queue pressure while the follow-up is busy.

## Acceptance criteria

- Retained promptable-idle sessions do not enter the stalled-worker watchdog or receive unsolicited grill prompts.
- The watchdog still detects genuinely in-flight work that has stopped making progress.
- Capacity reclamation can reclaim genuinely idle retained sessions, but never `busy`/prompt-in-flight sessions; re-check state immediately before stopping.
- Focused tests cover the default watchdog threshold, repeated watchdog ticks, cap-1 idle reclamation, and cap-1 busy follow-up pressure.
- The retained/promptable lifecycle is represented consistently enough that ACP status and durable task terminality cannot make cleanup, follow-up, and capacity consumers disagree.

Related: #28407, #28408.

1d:T73e,## Summary

Implements the progressive authorized content-access architecture for issue #26645. Model-facing content is preserved through typed references, exact bounded reads, revision-bound continuations, native source segments, central projection, final-wire admission, and explicit rejection instead of silent truncation.

The implementation covers files, documents, memory/message text, email/Gmail, attachments, and shell/tool output; adds deterministic six-family scale corpora; and unifies conformance, mutation, fault, scenario, E2E, PostgreSQL, benchmark, resource, cleanup, and soak evidence.

Design and research:
- docs/design/context-content/progressive-content-access-spec.md
- docs/design/context-content/progressive-content-access-research-and-test-report.md
- docs/design/context-content/progressive-content-access-goal.md

# Risks

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Establishes a canonical design-system boundary for product UI:

- Adds semantic token roles and restricts which component kinds may consume them.
- Adds canonical molecules for repeated structures: content states, settings rows, selectable tiles, action-list rows, and status badges.
- Migrates raw controls, duplicate atoms, deep imports, visual overrides, off-token colors, and equivalent recipes to their canonical owners.
- Uses a quiet 1 px semantic divider for table/list separation instead of boxed rows.
- Fixes compact landscape rendering, including centered selectable-tile copy without redundant `ON` text and the clipped white Today panel.
- Adds a fail-closed audit with ratcheted inventories so new violations cannot silently reappear.

## What kind of change is this?

Improvement and non-breaking feature.

## Why?

Visually equivalent controls had accumulated independent spacing, border, color, selection, and state treatments. That produced inconsistent tables and settings rows and made local fixes easy to regress. This change encodes the ownership rules in components, semantic tokens, inventories, and CI checks.

# Documentation changes needed?

Updated `packages/ui/DESIGN_SYSTEM.md`, the package guides, and generated design-system inventories.

21:T1d8c,## Summary

Telegram membership-authority vertical slice for #23101: the Telegram connector's join (`new_chat_members`) / leave (`left_chat_member`) / kick event paths and inbound group-message admission are wired into the canonical `MembershipService` authority (landed in #25379/#25474/#25514), proving same-turn revocation and fail-closed admission when the authority is stale, unavailable, or its writes fail.

Refs #23101 (kept open — this is the first connector slice of the multi-connector security architecture).

## What this PR does

- **`plugins/plugin-telegram/src/membership.ts`** — `TelegramMembershipAuthority`: per-(agent, account) authority client. Typed idempotent evidence commands from Telegram updates (deterministic idempotency keys, point-query evidence mode, TTL validity), out-of-order protection (strict same-second tie-break against resurrection), publisher fencing with adopt-and-retry on cursor/generation mismatches, bot-removal tombstones + re-add watermarks, restart hydration of persisted scope state, and a durable pending-revocation overlay for revocations whose evidence write failed.
- **`plugins/plugin-telegram/src/membership-gate.ts`** — the admission gate bound into `MessageManager`: every group message is gated BEFORE `processMessage`; denied principals reconcile via `getChatMember` and are re-checked; authority unavailability fails closed (`TELEGRAM_MEMBERSHIP_ENFORCE=1` strict, default degrade-allow+warn for non-sql deployments).
- **`plugins/plugin-telegram/src/service.ts` / `messageManager.ts`** — event wiring for join/leave/kick/bot-removal, connector-removal suspension, and the gate-before-processing ordering.
- **`plugins/plugin-sql/src/services/sql-membership.ts`** — one-line subject-scope fix in the reconcile path.

## Security invariants (all execution-proven, see tests)

1. **Same-turn revocation**: read → leave evidence → read denies (`membership_revoked`).
2. **Out-of-order/duplicate redelivery cannot resurrect** a committed revocation (strict same-second tie-break; distinct-message-id old joins skipped).
3. **Failed revocation writes fail closed**: the scope degrades stale AND a per-principal overlay fence installs atomically inside the same per-scope serialized chain link — a queued authorize cannot overtake the fence. The fence is durable (runtime cache) and survives restart even after unrelated evidence restores scope health.
4. **Cache outages fail closed**: an unreadable durable overlay denies admission (never consults possibly-stale active evidence); an unwritable fence escalates to gate-broken (every group admission fails closed).
5. **Never rewrites the durable overlay from an unhydrated set** — an unrelated evidence commit during a cache outage cannot erase other principals' revocation fences.
6. **Bot removal tombstones** the scope: backlogged evidence after removal cannot restore authority; only an explicit re-add (with a watermark suppressing pre-re-add observations) can.
7. **Reconcile subject-mismatch** (provider response describing a different user than requested) is rejected.

## Testing

Real-runtime PGlite suite (`vitest.real-runtime.config.ts`, real `AgentRuntime` + real `SqlMembershipService`, fault-injected outages on the real instances): **14/14 pass**, including:
- join/leave admission, getChatMember backfill reconcile, kicked reconcile deny
- duplicate redelivery + older-join non-resurrection + same-second tie-break
- restart publisher-binding continuation, restart tombstone hydration
- fault-injected pending revocation: overlay deny across unrelated-evidence health restore, **restart durability** (RED on parent: re-authorized), **queued-authorize overtake race** (RED on parent: admitted), **cache-outage fail-closed + recovery**, **unhydrated-set fence erasure** (restart scenario, RED on parent: fence erased).
- Every new regression was RED-control-proven on the parent commit (fix stashed) before being claimed as coverage.

Unit suites: membership gate/membership/bootstrap tests all pass; full `plugins/plugin-telegram` `bun test` shows only two failures, both proven identical at `origin/develop` control (pre-existing, untouched files).

Typecheck (`tsc -p`) clean; `biome check` clean.

22:Tbd1,Closes #26992.

## Outcome

Removes silent model-facing character, item-count, history, result, diagnostic, and output-token caps across runtime prompts, document reads/listing, connector message history/search, inbox/subscription scans, stored messages, attachments, action results, exports, diagnostics, and training paths.

The repository invariant is explicit and enforced: preserve complete context; use lossless ordered transport chunks; expose explicit caller pagination with truthful continuation; or reject before dispatch at a real external boundary. Do not summarize, compact, slice, retain only recent items, or invent output limits for model-facing content.

## Notable changes

- deletes automatic conversation compaction, `/compact`, and prompt-slicing utilities
- removes implicit provider/action/history/search/read caps across connector, document, inbox, browser, diagnostics, memory, and model paths
- exhausts provider cursors when callers omit pagination; explicit pagination remains caller-controlled and carries truthful continuation metadata
- keeps complete prompt/response logs, exports, task clipboard items, stored facts, and model outputs
- replaces externally enforced limits with typed reject-before-dispatch behavior instead of rewriting authored content
- rejects oversized training rows instead of truncating or guessing mask alignment
- adds a repository source guard against restoring reviewed caps, compaction modules, token truncation, or duplicate guard keys

## Reversion audit

The linked GitHub audit closed 44 cap-preserving or duplicate PRs, including follow-ups that only made an existing truncation Unicode-safe while retaining the data loss. UI-only previews, externally required protocol fields, explicit caller pagination, and lossless ordered chunking were retained after inspecting their actual consumers. Newly opened duplicate #28238 was also closed.

No deploy is included or authorized by this Draft PR.2a:T43a,## Summary

Successor to #27931. Makes world metadata authority writes compare-and-swap safe across core in-memory, plugin-inmemorydb, and SQL adapters.

- preserves CAS-managed roles and roleSources during connector-style ensureWorldExists calls
- retries concurrent legacy ensures against a fresh world snapshot and skips no-op writes
- rejects duplicate in-memory world creation instead of resetting revision and authority
- keeps typed stale-write errors, audit atomicity, and connector role CAS behavior
- runs SQL CAS and its role audit insert inside one entity-RLS-scoped transaction

Supersedes #27931.

## Focused verification

- core role-CAS and ensure-world tests: 14 passed
- plugin-inmemorydb world-CAS tests: 15 passed
- plugin-x utility tests: 7 passed
- core/plugin-inmemorydb/plugin-sql/plugin-x typechecks: passed
- touched-file Biome and git diff check: passed
- hosted static smoke, Windows security, and All Tests Passed: green on 1907036281074; fresh checks required for 260b6fecbe48d

Fresh independent review and exact-head checks are required before merge.2b:T1b8b,feat(core): durable per-principal capability grants, slice 1 (#23102)

## Summary

First implementation slice for #23102 per the issue's own slicing guidance: a durable subject × capability × resource grant store and the canonical fail-closed `authorizeCapability` decision path, in a new `packages/core/src/features/capabilities/` module. Storage lives in a dedicated `capabilities` Postgres schema, self-materialized through idempotent DDL on first store use (the TrajectoriesService pattern) because no registered plugin carries a schema-bearing entry — relying on plugin-schema migration would leave the tables uncreated in real deployments.

- **Typed, versioned vocabulary** (`types.ts`): canonical subjects (`entity:<uuid>`, `role:<name>`, `world:<uuid>`, case-folded), dot-separated lowercase capabilities, strict selector grammar (`*`, exact ids, trailing `/*` only — printable ASCII allowlist, no leading/trailing/double slashes, length cap). Malformed input is rejected at the write (error-policy J3); legacy rows that drift are quarantined — visible for management, never authoritative.
- **Store** (`CapabilityGrantStore.ts`): validated create, version-checked update, idempotent revoke — each mutation and its global `capability_epoch` bump commit in ONE transaction; a missing epoch singleton rolls the mutation back instead of fabricating progress. Uncapped live-grant reads filtered (expiry `> now`, non-revoked) in SQL. Concurrent-safe promise-memoized DDL bootstrap.
- **Decision path** (`authorizeCapability.ts`): fixed evaluation order — validate (fail closed) → explicit deny grants → allow grants (constraints intersected: numeric min, typed scalar array intersection, structural equality; incompatible → deny) → role-tier composition hook → deny `NO_MATCHING_GRANT` (no implicit allow). No decision cache, so revocation is effective before the next call returns by construction. Every decision attempts exactly one audit row (id generated up front); audit failures are reported through an injected `onAuditFailure` hook (J7; wire to `runtime.reportError` at the boundary) and can never block or flip a decision. Audit rows carry categorical/sanitized fields only — raw malformed values and driver errors never enter the log.
- **Enforcement inventory** (`capability-boundary-inventory.ts`): 12 privileged boundaries (message send/read, device, fs read/write, PTy, git, media read/write, provider invoke, admin API, coding environments) with per-boundary status; the shape is test-validated. Completeness against future repo boundaries is a human-review obligation until the slice-2 classifier.
- Exported node-only from `index.node.ts` (SQL-bound; browser/edge untouched).

## Test evidence

`bun run --cwd packages/core test` scope: **41/41 passing** real-PGlite tests in `CapabilityGrantStore.test.ts` (default vitest lane; PGlite disk data dirs, real Drizzle queries, no mocks of the system under test):

- Self-migration from an empty database; concurrent first calls share one bootstrap
- Restart survival: handle closed, fresh handle opened on the SAME disk dir — grants (and deny grants) created under the first handle remain authoritative; epoch identical
- Revocation visible to the very next decision; expiry boundary `expiresAt <= now` never matches
- Deny-wins over simultaneous allows and over the role tier; role tier never rescues a deny; broken resolver degrades to deny
- Fail-closed: malformed subject/capability/agentId/resource/worldId → `INVALID_REQUEST`; store down → `STORE_UNAVAILABLE`; no grant → `NO_MATCHING_GRANT`
- Audit: per-decision rows, categorical details (raw values withheld), 512-char resource truncation, failure reported through the hook, throwing reporter cannot abort any decision path
- Optimistic-version conflicts; partial unique index (world-aware via nil-uuid coalescing) blocks duplicate active policies and frees on revoke; identical policies legal in distinct worlds and alongside global
- Typed constraint intersection incl. non-scalar arrays → incompatible; nearest expiry across multiple allows
- Quarantine of legacy rows with malformed selector/issuer/capability/subject/effect/provenance
- Mutation rollback when the epoch singleton is missing (zero grant rows survive)
- Boundary inventory shape validation

Typecheck clean (`bun run --cwd packages/core typecheck`), Biome clean, `bun run --cwd packages/core build` succeeds and the built barrel exports `authorizeCapability` + store API (verified by import probe against the built output).

## Review evidence

Independently reviewed by a second agent (RepoPrompt CE, gpt-5.6-sol) over the full embedded diff across **5 rounds**: 16 must-fix findings raised, all fixed with direct test coverage — including removal of a 500-row live-grant cap that could break deny-wins, audit sanitization + injected failure reporting, selector charset grammar, transactional epoch bumps with rollback, promise-memoized concurrent migration, world-aware uniqueness, typed/structural constraint semantics, and forced-invocation reporter tests. Final round passed all six checklist areas; the sole residual (a header wording nit) is fixed in 87a4f61.

Closes #23102 (slice 1 of the tracked follow-ups).

# Risks

Low. All changes are fail-closed hardening of the entity-resolution parse boundary in `packages/core`. The behavioral risk direction is over-rejection (resolution returns null where it previously resolved): the byte-identical preservation corpus (33 cases, every shape that resolved on `origin/develop`) guards against that. No schema, storage, or API-surface changes; no migration; no plugin contract change.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Fixes the three fail-open/data-loss paths in `packages/core/src/entities.ts` from the issue, then hardens the parse boundary itself:

1. **AMBIGUOUS/UNKNOWN are terminal.** Diagnostic `matches` on those types can no longer resolve an entity; only the contract's decisive types (`EXACT_MATCH`, `USERNAME_MATCH`, `NAME_MATCH`, `RELATIONSHIP_MATCH`) may resolve at all.
2. **Decisive evidence must be consistent.** Every SUPPLIED identification field — `entityId`/`resolvedId` for any decisive type, and every match entry — must resolve to exactly one id-bearing in-scope candidate, or the whole response is unresolved:
   - a supplied non-string id alias invalidates **per field** (`entityId: 42` next to a valid `resolvedId` no longer resolves);
   - contradictory `entityId`/`resolvedId` strings invalidate;
   - malformed match evidence can no longer vanish at the parse boundary: `matches: null`, a `{match: 42}` wrapper, wrapped or direct arrays containing dropped entries (`[{name:"Alice"}, {}]`, holes), scalars, and name-less/reason-only entries all invalidate — legality is now decided by a strict variant of the normalizer walk (`normalizeEntityMatchesStrict` in `entity-matches.ts`) that counts every dropped supplied slot, so the boundary rules cannot drift from the walk's semantics;
   - a match label naming zero, several (duplicate-name), id-less, or out-of-scope candidates invalidates instead of silently resolving to the first index hit;
   - exact contextual `me`/`myself`/`you`/`yourself` referents bind to the sender/agent before any ordinary identity lookup, so an entity literally named "Me"/"You" cannot capture them (and if the contextual target is not in scope, resolution fails rather than falling through).
3. **Id-less room entities are an integrity error.** `getEntityDetails` throws a typed `ElizaError` (`ROOM_ENTITY_ID_MISSING`) instead of silently handing downstream model context a shortened roster.

`undefined` remains the only absent encoding for `matches`; JSON `null` stays the documented absent encoding for `entityId` (per the resolver template contract) but is malformed for `matches` (documented as an array).

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes (non-breaking change which fixes an issue).

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

# Risks

Low. Pin management is metadata-only: it changes provider injection priority and
never touches authorization decisions. The adapter contract change is additive
(`updateDocumentPinned` + `documentPinCapability`), with a typed
`DOCUMENT_PIN_UNSUPPORTED` error surfaced as 503 for legacy adapters instead of
a fabricated success.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Implements the first production slice of #23103:

1. **Pin/unpin/list through service + REST + agent action.** `DocumentService.setDocumentPinned` performs a CAS-protected metadata mutation through the existing authorization + revision fencing plus the dedicated `expectedPinned` pin-bit fence (review r1: two racing pin writers from the same observed state now serialize — exactly one `updated`, the loser gets a typed conflict and the service's bounded retry converges against fresh state; no second write path). REST: `POST /api/documents/:id/pin` / `DELETE /api/documents/:id/pin` behind the existing mutate gate, mapping typed `DOCUMENT_PIN_UNSUPPORTED` to 503. Agent surface: `pin`/`unpin` subactions on the DOCUMENT umbrella action. The presented-document wire shape now carries `pinned`, and the Documents view shows a visible per-row pending state with a fail-closed unsupported-adapter signal.
2. **Post-#24134 prompt-integrity policy on pinned provider injection.** The deterministic 8,000-token pinned-injection cap (first-N selection + `[PINNED KNOWLEDGE TRUNCATED]` marker) is gone: every authorized selected pinned document reaches the model completely. Provider composition exhausts pagination before selecting pinned documents.

**Coordination with upstream #26780/#28112:** during review, upstream landed prompt-integrity work touching the same provider seam (`renderPinnedDocuments` now returns `{text, truncated: boolean, includedIds}` and callers expose `pinnedDocumentsTruncated` as the completeness signal, always `false`). This branch concedes that seam wholesale to upstream (100% upstream bytes for `provider.ts` + its two test files) and keeps the complete-injection policy through upstream's own shape. An independent integration review (round 5) verified no silent cap/clip/marker remains, no semantic conflict with #28112's rewritten limit semantics in the auto-merged files, and that no required deliverable was dropped.

Explicit non-goals (follow-up slices of #23103): durable entitlement grants/sharing flows (#23102 coordination), multi-room pin scoping UI.

## What kind of change is this?

Features (non-breaking change which adds functionality)

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation. (The plugin-documents `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` pair is updated in-branch for the new pin routes.)

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`packages/core/src/features/documents/__tests__/actions-pin.test.ts` (agent action wiring), then `plugins/plugin-documents/src/routes.pin.test.ts` (REST + 503 mapping), then `plugins/plugin-inmemorydb/document-pin.test.ts` + `plugins/plugin-sql/src/__tests__/integration/document-pin.real.test.ts` (real adapter persistence, CAS + concurrency).

## Detailed testing steps

- Pin/unpin via the DOCUMENT umbrella action; assert metadata-only mutation through existing authorization (never a second write path).
- `POST /api/documents/:id/pin` on a legacy adapter (inherited base method) — expect 503 typed `DOCUMENT_PIN_UNSUPPORTED`, never a fabricated success.
- Concurrent pin toggles on the same document — REAL two-writer SQL proof on PGlite (`document-pin.real.test.ts`): both writers read before either writes, race `Promise.all` CAS writes; exactly one `updated` + one `conflict`, and the loser's retry against fresh state converges. RED control: removing the `expectedPinned` fence makes both writers report `updated` (lost update) and the test fails.
- Pinned provider injection renders complete content (no budget, no marker).

2e:T128e,# feat(core): atomic world-metadata CAS for role writes (#23100)

Closes #23100 (bounded tranche: atomic CAS/versioned role writes on world metadata + durable audit records).

## What this does

Implements compare-and-swap semantics for world-metadata role writes so concurrent role changes can't silently clobber each other, with a durable audit log row committed atomically with each swap.

- `compareAndSwapWorldMetadata` on `IDatabaseAdapter`: read → compare → audit insert → world replace, all serialized on a storage-scoped mutation tail
- Audit row insert precedes the world write; a failed world write compensates by deleting the audit row (no false committed records — error-policy J6)
- Storage-scoped serialization (WeakMap tail keyed on the storage instance) covers every adapter over a shared storage AND every world-collection write path: `createWorlds`, `deleteWorlds`, `updateWorlds`, `upsertWorlds`, and the CAS itself
- Unknown privilege-bearing legacy strings fail closed after centralized normalization
- SQL adapter base implements the same contract; inmem adapter exercises it end-to-end

## Why the tail is storage-scoped

The plugin shares one `MemoryStorage` singleton across adapter instances in a process. An adapter-local lock would let two adapters (or a legacy `updateWorlds` writer racing a CAS) both compare the same snapshot and both "win". Attaching the serialization to the shared storage object closes both holes.

## Regression tests (write-gate handshake)

The resurrection/clobber tests pin the exact interleaving with an entered/release handshake on the CAS's world write: the delete/create actor provably lands between the CAS read and its write. RED control by execution: reverting ONLY the createWorlds/deleteWorlds tail routing makes exactly these two tests fail; restoring returns 12/12 green.

## Verification

- Inmem CAS suite: 12/12 (snapshot match/mismatch, key-order value equality, audit placement + J6 rollback, not-found, cross-adapter serialization, live-reference hazard, resurrection, clobber)
- Full inmem suite: 90/90 green
- SQL adapter (real): 184 passed / 3 skipped
- Core: 20 failures identical (python-set-diffed) to pristine develop de774b87 — all pre-existing (evaluator/budget/cache tests), zero attributable to this PR
- typecheck clean; biome clean
- Independent RP review rounds 1-7 (gpt-5.6-sol): rounds 5-6 drove the write-gate handshake + awaited-deletion fixes into the regression tests; round 7 APPROVED at head b308a61e90

## Out of scope (per issue's wave pattern)

Principal-kind separation, complete gate inventory, UI transitions — separate tranches per the worker notes; this PR is the CAS + audit tranche only.

2f:T54a7,On `develop`, a caller cannot trust complete-retrieval traversal. A document request whose vector recall is empty still comes back carrying keyword-corpus rows that never matched the query. A `plugin-inmemorydb` traversal keeps re-serving page one and then rejects a healthy source. A growing-prefix PII walk aborts a stable source with `ElizaError: PII context memories source changed during traversal`.

Those are the failure classes @lalalune reproduced in #25150. Verbatim:

> 1. Hybrid document search can add irrelevant keyword-corpus rows when vector results are empty or sparse.
> 2. The first-party plugin-inmemorydb adapter does not honor the widened stable offset contract, so complete traversal repeats the first page and rejects healthy sources.
> 3. Growing-prefix PII traversal reranks each prefix with BM25, so a stable source can appear unstable as corpus statistics change.
> 4. Deterministic overlapping document pages can pass double-scan equality while duplicating one row and omitting another.
> 5. Requiring two whole-corpus scans to match rejects ordinary concurrent prepends/appends instead of traversing a stable keyset snapshot.

Refs #25150. **This PR does not close it.** It reproduces all five classes as failing tests and makes them pass, but a CHANGES_REQUESTED review found six defects — four P1 — in the fix itself, and none of them are fixed yet. The per-item dispositions are below; every one is conceded. I am keeping the PR open with the blockers enumerated rather than re-requesting review.

### 2 — plugin-inmemorydb ignores the paging contract

## Review findings — per-item disposition

Against [`startrack3r`'s CHANGES_REQUESTED review](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/pull/28336#pullrequestreview-5016829589) (static review of `9197af5`). I re-read each cited location at `d4b8d5a`. **All six still stand. All six are conceded. None are fixed.**

**1. [P1] RLS principal is bound to a result filter — CONCEDED, OPEN.** `plugins/plugin-sql/src/base.ts:4043` still reads `return this.withEntityContext(params.entityId ?? null, async (tx) => {` inside `searchMemoriesPage`, while `params.entityId` is applied separately as a row filter at the `eq(memoryTable.entityId, params.entityId)` condition. The requester principal is `params.accessContext?.requesterEntityId`. Under PostgreSQL RLS this makes a row filter drive `current_entity_id()`, and an omitted filter supplies no principal at all. The reviewer is also right that the existing real-SQL test uses one UUID for both roles and therefore cannot catch it. Fix owed: bind `withEntityContext` to the validated requester with an explicit absent-context policy, plus real-Postgres cases where requester A filters entity B and where the context is missing or empty.

**2. [P1] Shared in-memory storage is not agent-isolated — CONCEDED, OPEN.** `plugins/plugin-inmemorydb/adapter.ts:1375` `searchMemoriesPage` filters on `tableName`, `roomId`, `worldId`, `entityId`, and `unique`, with no `memory.agentId === this.agentId` predicate. The plugin shares one `MemoryStorage` across adapters and the dimension rebuild loads every stored agent into the index, and the PII path calls this without an `AccessContext`. Another agent's rows can therefore reach the result, the count, and the snapshot fingerprint. Fix owed: enforce the agent boundary in both the stable and legacy paths, scope index rebuild per agent, and test two adapters sharing one storage across a rebuild.

**3. [P1] This is not storage-level keyset paging — CONCEDED, OPEN, and it is the acceptance criterion.** Confirmed at head: `searchMemoriesPage` issues `.orderBy(asc(distance), desc(memoryTable.createdAt), desc(memoryTable.id))` with no cursor predicate and no `LIMIT`, then filters, maps, and hands the complete ordered corpus to `createStableRetrievalPage`, which slices and fingerprints it in JavaScript. The two document queries do the same. Every page is O(N) transfer, sort, and hash; a traversal is O(N × pages). The prior body disclosed this as a "tradeoff" in *Scope and risk* while ticking the adapter-keyset acceptance box in the same document — that contradiction is withdrawn and the box is now unticked. Fix owed: push the exclusive `(score, createdAt, id)` continuation and the snapshot fence into each adapter query with `LIMIT pageSize + 1`, plus query-shape and large-corpus assertions.

**4. [P1] Torn in-memory storage/index state — CONCEDED, OPEN.** I confirmed at head that `searchMemoriesPage` guards only with `withDocumentMutationLock`, which chains on `documentMutationTail` (`plugins/plugin-inmemorydb/adapter.ts:872`). The reviewer's point stands: ordinary create/update/delete write storage and the vector index as separate awaited operations outside that tail, and clearing an embedding leaves the HNSW node behind — I have not independently re-derived that second half. A page can rank against stale vector state, return a differently-shaped stored row, and still mint a snapshot over it. Fix owed: serialize those mutations against stable reads (or publish an atomic immutable snapshot), drop the index node when an embedding is cleared, and add forced-interleaving regressions.

**5. Backward-compatibility regressions — CONCEDED, OPEN, both halves.** (a) `plugins/plugin-inmemorydb/adapter.ts:1320` still has `const threshold = params.match_threshold ?? -1;` on the legacy `searchMemories` path, changing the omitted-threshold default from `0.5` and handing existing callers low- and negative-similarity rows. Restoring `0.5` with tests for omitted / explicit `0` / explicit `-1` is owed. (b) `packages/core/src/types/runtime.ts:644,647` declare `searchMemoriesPage` and `rerankMemories` as required `IAgentRuntime` members while the runtime feature-detects them as optional — that breaks structurally typed third-party runtimes. Owed: model them as optional or versioned capability members, or move them to an extended interface.

**6. Stable-helper cross-adapter gaps — CONCEDED, OPEN.** Confirmed at head in `packages/core/src/database/stable-retrieval.ts`: `buildSnapshot` compares `key.id > upperId` as a raw case-sensitive string while the canonical adapter comparator lowercases to match PostgreSQL, and `fingerprint()` hashes only `id`, `createdAt`, `similarity`, `content`, `embedding`, `metadata`, `roomId`, `worldId`, `entityId` — so a mutation to a returned field it omits, such as `unique`, passes the old snapshot unchanged. Owed: canonicalize UUID comparison and duplicate identity, fingerprint the complete canonical returned row, and add parity/mutation tests.

**7. Overstated end-to-end claim — CONCEDED and corrected here.** The reviewer is right that the projection case is not the real end-to-end path the previous body advertised. `packages/core/src/security/issue-25150-runtime-adversarial.test.ts:124` constructs a real `DocumentService`, but the adapter beneath it is `queryDocumentFragments: vi.fn(async () => fragments)` over 1,205 synthetic rows, with a mocked runtime around it. It proves the projection seam does not silently truncate; it does not prove a real DocumentService → storage → planner path. The "real `DocumentService` → projection → `AgentRuntime` dispatch" wording is withdrawn.

**8. Evidence-row markers — FIXED.** All seven mandatory evidence-row markers plus the OCR row were missing from the previous body and are restored below, and the evidence-head marker now carries `d4b8d5a`.

**9. Rebase onto live `develop` — PARTIALLY DONE.** Rebased onto `f2ccf25` at `d4b8d5a`, preserving both sides of the `plugin-inmemorydb` conflict with #28112 (`const limit = params.count ?? params.limit;` from develop, `const threshold = params.match_threshold ?? -1;` from this PR — and per finding 5 that second line is itself owed a revert). `develop` has since advanced 6 commits; another rebase is owed.

## Acceptance status against #25150

Honest mapping. Nothing is ticked that the rest of this body contradicts.

- [x] Add current-develop real-service/adversarial tests for all five cases and prove each expected-success path — 13 focused core tests, 1 in-memory adapter test, 4 real-SQL tests, each failing on the pre-fix tree and passing at `d4b8d5a`.
- [x] Preserve relevance: query-disjoint rows never enter model context merely because a complete source traversal exists — the empty-vector and sparse-vector disjoint-corpus cases pass.
- [ ] **Not met.** Every first-party adapter implements the same stable ordered keyset/cursor contract — the *cursor semantics* are enforced and ignored cursors are rejected, but per finding 3 the SQL adapters do not issue a keyset query; they materialize the full corpus and page it in JavaScript.
- [x] Traversal detects duplicate, missing, reordered, repeating, and no-progress pages without reranking incompatible prefixes — five page-integrity vectors plus the PII single-rerank case pass.
- [ ] **Partially met.** Stable snapshot contract tolerating concurrent appends or returning a typed conflict — `RETRIEVAL_SNAPSHOT_CONFLICT` with `action: restart-from-first-page` works for the tested vectors, but findings 4 and 6 mean the snapshot can be minted over torn in-memory state and can miss mutations to unfingerprinted fields.
- [ ] **Stale evidence.** PGLite and real PostgreSQL exercise the same semantic vectors — proved at `9197af5`; only the PGLite-lane real-SQL file has a recorded pass at `d4b8d5a`, and the PostgreSQL 18 lane has not been re-run since the rebase.
- [ ] **Overstated, corrected.** Integrate with #24592 so complete retrieval cannot later be hidden by prompt projection — the projection seam is covered over a mocked adapter (finding 7), not the real retrieval path.
- [ ] **Not met.** Focused tests, typecheck/build/lint, root verify, exact-head CI, and independent review pass — focused tests, biome and typecheck pass at `d4b8d5a`; root `verify`, builds and lints are stale at `9197af5`; exact-head CI is red; the independent review is CHANGES_REQUESTED and unanswered in code.

## Scope and risk

Risk: medium, and higher than the previous body admitted. `IDatabaseAdapter` / `IAgentRuntime` gain stable-page methods and a capability marker; findings 1, 2 and 5 mean the current shape of those additions changes an authorization principal, crosses an agent boundary, and moves a public default. Legacy adapters take the corrected single-rerank path; first-party adapters take the native contract.

Snapshot verification fingerprints the complete fenced authorized corpus on every page. That makes mutation detection deterministic and is why the tests pass, but it is O(N × pages) over a traversal and is exactly what finding 3 rejects. No scalability improvement is claimed anywhere in this PR.

No documentation change is required: this changes the database/runtime contract and its exported public types, not operator-facing setup.

<!-- This risks section must be filled out before the final review and merge. -->

# Risks
Medium. Push delivery semantics change for multi-user agents: legacy tokens registered without an owner never receive pushes until re-registered; a principal with no saved policy gets inbox-only (fail-closed by design). Inbox delivery itself is unchanged. Rollback is a revert; no migration/schema change.

<!--
Low, medium, large. List what kind of risks and what could be affected.
-->

# Background
Notifications were agent-scoped: every push fan-fanned out to every registered token regardless of recipient, and there was no per-principal policy seam. Per the maintainer disposition, this tranche (1) makes notifications carry a canonical recipient and binds push delivery to it, and (2) adds an inbox-before-push policy seam with a fail-closed decision. Digests stay with the core scheduler architecture (one-clock invariant; AGENTS.md "Scheduling and personal-assistant domains") — no competing scheduler is introduced.

## What does this PR do?

- **Canonical recipient**: additive `recipientId` on `AgentNotification`/`NotificationInput` (explicit > canonical owner > absent — never fabricated, never agentId). Push delivery goes through `listByOwner`; the agent-scoped fan-out is removed. Legacy unowned tokens hydrate/list/unregister but never receive pushes.
- **Inbox-before-push policy seam**: new `packages/agent/src/services/push/push-policy.ts` — durable per-principal `PushDeliveryPolicy` + pure `decidePushDelivery` matrix: no recipient / no policy / corrupt policy / explicit deny → inbox-only; only explicit `pushEnabled: true` pushes.
- **Exact-shape untrusted-row validation**: `parsePushDeliveryPolicy` requires the row's own enumerable key set to equal exactly `{pushEnabled, version, updatedAt}` — extra keys fail closed and inherited (prototype) values are not substitutes (blocks `Object.create` inherited-policy injection; RED-controlled regression test).
- **Typed persist errors**: `PushPolicyStore.save` throws `ElizaError` (code `PUSH_POLICY_PERSIST_FAILED`, context, severity) instead of plain `Error`.
- **Routes**: `POST /api/notifications/push-tokens` binds owner (body ids accepted only when equal to the canonical owner; malformed/null → 400); `GET/PUT /api/notifications/push-policy` (409 without canonical owner); remote-forwarder forwards the new policy mutations.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes (privacy: recipient-bound push) + Features (per-principal push policy seam). Non-breaking: `recipientId` is additive; unowned legacy tokens degrade to inbox-only by design.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`packages/agent/src/services/push/push-policy.ts` (policy + pure decision matrix), then `push-token-registry.ts` (`listByOwner`), then `notification-push-service.ts` (recipient-bound delivery), then `packages/core/src/services/notification.ts` (recipient stamping), then the tests in the same directories.

## Detailed testing steps

1. `bun test packages/agent/src/services/push/push-policy.test.ts packages/agent/src/services/push/notification-push-service.test.ts packages/agent/src/services/push/push-token-registry.test.ts packages/agent/src/api/push-token-routes.test.ts packages/agent/src/services/push/push-registration-flow.test.ts` → 91/91 pass.

4. Recipient stamping matrix: `packages/core/src/services/notification.test.ts` (present/absent/padded recipient; never agentId).

```bash
                                          (re-open the exact matrix run)
  bun run --cwd packages/app audit:app    (app + cloud UI — REQUIRED for UI changes)
```

The matrix command creates and verifies one named bundle, then passes that
exact run to the reviewer. Do not replace the `--bundle` path with raw producer
directories; `--source` is only for explicit archived/ad-hoc compatibility.

A UI-touching diff (rendered `.tsx`/`.css`/`.svg` under `packages/app`,
`packages/ui`, `apps/app`, …) MUST attach real screenshot/video/OCR artifacts —
the CI evidence gate rejects `N/A` on those rows for such diffs, label or not.
If a capture tool is missing, `evidence:doctor` prints the install command;
install it rather than marking evidence `N/A`.

## What

Adds a new unit suite for the raw-SQL approval state machine: `packages/agent/src/services/approval/store.test.ts` (1885 lines, +N/-0, no production files touched).

The suite drives the exported `PgApprovalQueue` / `createApprovalQueue` directly through the real `runtime.adapter.db.execute` boundary, so the store's actual SQL encoders (`sqlText`/`sqlJson`), row parsers, and transition guards run unmodified. The only substitution is a shim for drizzle's `sql.raw`, letting an in-memory fake of the `approval_requests` table interpret the INSERT/SELECT/UPDATE/DELETE shapes the store emits.

## Coverage

- construction and option handling
- enqueue + idempotent replay (including conflict errors)
- enqueue payload validation per action (`send_message`, `schedule_event`, `spend_money`, ...)
- row parsing: unknown states/actions/channels, missing/invalid timestamps, malformed metadata
- queries and ownership fences: newest-first ordering, filter combinations, LIMIT semantics (unbounded, explicit page size, `LIMIT 0`)
- resolution transitions with lost-race detection (`ApprovalStateTransitionError`) and lazy expiry at the transition boundary
- execution lifecycle: claim, retryable failure vs reconciliation required, receipt preservation/replacement encoding, unstarted-claim recovery
- purgeExpired
- notification side channels and awaited grouped projection (exact unread-equality predicate), including refusal before insert when the notification service cannot project

No deploy is included or authorized by this Draft PR.33:T179b,## Summary

Implements the first lane of #23105 per lalalune's maintainer disposition (versioned directional content-delivery matrix + one real Discord→Telegram text/file row; live-credential acceptance is a later lane):

- **Versioned matrix** (`packages/scenario-runner/src/content-delivery-matrix/`): schema (v1) with closed row field set, a fail-closed compiler that validates and freezes rows (unique ids, enum checks, self-pair rejection, transform-class-driven proof obligations), and a completeness gate (`assertDeliveryCoverage`) that throws when a declared capability has no covering row. The registry compiles **fail-closed at module load** — an uncovered capability throws on first import.
- **One real row pair** (`discord-to-telegram.text` / `.file`): the wire suite drives plugin-telegram's REAL `MessageManager.sendMessageInChunks` → telegraf HTTP against the repository mock fleet, and core's REAL SSRF-guarded `fetchRemoteMedia` for the inbound leg. Proofs are typed provider receipts + SHA-256 byte equality + provider readback — never prose.
- **Proof-producing certification**: assertion wrappers (`certifiedReceipt`, `certifiedVerbatimText`, `certifiedBytePreserving`, `certifiedReadback`) discharge a proof kind only because the wrapped assertion ran and passed; the binding test compares executed certifications against every registry row's `requiredProofs`. RED control executed: removing a certification call fails the suite.
- **Mock fleet extensions**: Discord CDN-style byte serving (register + GET with digest), Telegram multipart upload with byte-level readback routes, URL-media fetch policy-gated to fleet-bound loopback origins (timeout, no redirects, 10 MiB cap, J1 boundary translation of every failure), and a strict multipart parser (delimiter-LINE framing, part/scan budgets, filename required) with a 7-test adversarial unit suite.

36:T25ad,Fixes #23104 (bounded first delivery-truthfulness slice)

## Summary

First bounded slice of #23104: connector send handlers derive one structural `SendHandlerOutcome` per logical send instead of `void`/throw, and iMessage attempts **every** requested attachment instead of silently selecting only the first. Slack attachment failures are no longer swallowed after text succeeds — they surface as typed per-part evidence.

### Slack (`plugins/plugin-slack`)

- `handleSendMessage` returns a typed `SendHandlerOutcome` (`delivered` / `partially_delivered` / `not_delivered`) composed from text chunk `ts` receipts plus per-attachment upload evidence, in provider send order.
- `sendOutboundAttachments` returns per-part evidence: successful uploads carry the `files.uploadV2` file id; failed parts carry a stable `code` + `message`. An upload resolving without a file id is treated as a failed part — never fabricated into a receipt.
- Empty content → `not_delivered` (`SLACK_EMPTY_MESSAGE`) instead of throwing; nothing accepted → `SLACK_ATTACHMENT_DELIVERY_FAILED`; subset failure → `SLACK_ATTACHMENT_PARTIAL_DELIVERY` with the failed subset enumerated.

### iMessage (`plugins/plugin-imessage`)

- `sendMessage` accepts `mediaUrls: string[]`; the HTTP route still accepts legacy singular `mediaUrl` (ordered first when both are present).
- Every requested attachment is resolved and byte-bounded through the SSRF-guarded fetcher **before** the first external send — unusable parts fail the whole send fast, with all staged dirs cleaned up.
- Mid-send failures return delivered-part evidence (`textChunks`, `attachments`, per-part local effect stamps) so callers do not blind-retry and duplicate externally delivered parts; the sendHandler surfaces it as `partially_delivered` (`IMESSAGE_PARTIAL_DELIVERY`).
- Staged attachment dirs are cleaned best-effort on every exit path (error-policy:J6): a cleanup rejection can never convert a delivered send into a thrown failure.
- Route + sendHandler empty-input paths return `not_delivered` outcomes with stable codes instead of throwing.

### Out of scope (explicitly deferred)

The issue's authorization-bearing Media references and remaining connectors (WhatsApp/Telegram/Discord matrix) are not in this slice; this PR takes the issue-body-sanctioned bounded slice (truthful multi-attachment delivery for two connectors). Follow-up slices will cover authorization + remaining connectors.

## Root-cause verification

Issue author's live-source disposition (2026-08-21) named three symptoms. All three verified against pristine develop before implementing:

1. **Slack swallows attachment failure**: develop's `sendOutboundAttachments` catches fetch/upload errors, logs a warning, returns `void` — a text-then-failed-attachments send reported success with no per-part record.
2. **iMessage first-attachment-only**: develop's outbound selection is `attachments?.find(...)` — one attachment max, silently dropping the rest.
3. **Void/throw handlers**: both `sendHandler`s returned `Promise<void>` or threw, erasing delivered-part evidence on partial failure.

An RP (RepoPrompt) investigation session independently confirmed root cause and files/lines before implementation; RP review r1 (REQUEST_CHANGES, 8 findings) and r2 re-review of the applied fixes returned **APPROVE** with 4 P3 non-blocking notes.

## Evidence

### Test evidence (inline)

Two new suites pin the contract; both fail on pristine develop (RED control: Slack 8/8 fail, iMessage 5/6 fail — the 6th exercises pre-existing resolve-fail-fast behavior that develop already had):

```
plugin-slack   src/handle-send-outcome.test.ts    8 tests — composed outcomes:
  delivered (ordered ts+fileId receipt), partially_delivered (text ok + all
  attachments fail; subset-fail enumeration), not_delivered (attachments-only
  all-fail, nothing accepted), file-id-only receipt for attachments-only
  sends, missing-file-id = failed part never fabricated, whitespace-only URL
  attempted + reported as failure never silently skipped, empty content =
  stable SLACK_EMPTY_MESSAGE code
plugin-imessage src/outbound-multi-attachment.test.ts  6 tests — all parts
  attempted, resolve-before-send (0 text + 0 attachment sends on resolve
  fail), cleanup-after-late-failure (3/3 staged dirs incl. never-sent third),
  delivered-part evidence on mid-send failure
```

# Deploy Notes

No deploy is included or authorized by this Draft PR.38:T2c51,`bun run knip` cannot produce a dead-code result on this repository. At `6cc570c8efa9000a163df5b37487898551c64d29` it ran ~24 minutes with the configured `NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size=16384`, climbed to ~16.34 GiB heap, and aborted with `FATAL ERROR: Ineffective mark-compacts near heap limit` — exit 134.

So the canonical audit does not return findings a caller can act on; it returns nothing at all, after 24 minutes. The failure and the acceptance criteria below are @lalalune's own, filed as #27201.

This PR makes that command complete under a 4096 MiB per-process ceiling without adding a single ignore.

# Why not a smaller fix

Three cheaper fixes were considered and rejected before writing a runner.

**Raise the heap past 16 GiB.** The reproduction is not a machine that is slightly too small. It is a single Knip process building one program over the entire repository: 152 authoritative workspaces. Raising the ceiling moves the wall, keeps the ~24-minute wall-clock, and makes the command unrunnable on any contributor machine with less RAM than the one that filed the issue. It also leaves the failure mode unchanged — an OOM still reads as "no result", and #27201 is explicit that this "must not be treated as a clean result."

**Add ignores until it fits.** This is the fix #27201 forbids by name: "*do not silence findings with broad ignores merely to reduce memory*", and "*Preserve visibility of cross-workspace exports and dynamic/public entrypoints*". Ignoring workspaces to shrink the program deletes exactly the cross-workspace edges the audit exists to find. `knip.json` is **not** in this PR's diff — the four changed files are `package.json` and three new files under `packages/scripts/`. No ignore, `ignoreDependencies`, or `ignoreWorkspaces` entry was added or widened.

**A plain `for workspace in ...; do knip; done` loop.** This is the shape #9728 (merged 2026-06-25, "memory-bounded per-package knip + dead-file verifier") already took, and it is why #27201 is still open. A per-package loop is memory-bounded but wrong: analyzing a workspace in isolation makes every export it publishes to a *sibling* look unused, so the aggregate fills with false positives and any real finding is unfindable. It also cannot fail correctly — a loop that skips, double-counts, or OOMs one package still prints a green summary.

The partition runner is the smallest thing that is bounded **and** keeps cross-workspace visibility **and** cannot go green on a partial result.

# What it does

`packages/scripts/knip-partitioned.mjs` keeps one logical owner partition per authoritative workspace, and runs each owner's *graph context* — small batches of its real dependents, discovered from manifests and from actual TypeScript/JavaScript import, `export from`, dynamic-import and `require` specifiers — as sequential Knip shards from the repository root. Consumer-sensitive findings (unused exports/files) survive only if present in **every** successful dependent shard; positive findings (cycles, unresolved imports) are unioned. Root and per-workspace Knip config is composed, not replaced, so public entrypoints and dynamic-use exceptions are preserved; the exact effective config per workspace is written out for review.

Every child process is spawned with `NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size=4096` (`OLD_SPACE_CEILING_MIB = 4096`, `knip-partitioned.mjs:38`, applied at `:899`).

`knip` stays advisory for findings but fails on any infrastructure failure. `knip:strict` runs the identical analysis and additionally fails when the aggregate is non-empty. `infrastructureFailure()` classifies OOM, signals, non-zero exits, and missing/malformed JSON, so a partial run cannot present as clean.

Reports land in `reports/knip/`: `aggregate.json` (deterministic ledger + outcomes + findings), `run.json` (commands, digests, durations, exit codes, signals, stderr, ceiling), `workspaces/*.json`, `configs/*.json`.

# Reproduction

**Before**, at issue head `6cc570c8efa9000a163df5b37487898551c64d29`:

```console
$ NODE_OPTIONS='--max-old-space-size=16384' knip --no-progress --no-exit-code
[52782:0x86040c000]   590101 ms: Mark-Compact (reduce) 16289.0 (16423.7) -> 16288.1 (16307.5) MB, ...
FATAL ERROR: Ineffective mark-compacts near heap limit Allocation failed - JavaScript heap out of memory
error: script "knip" exited with code 134
      590.20 real       486.82 user       201.26 sys
          5380276224  maximum resident set size
```

**After**, at this PR's head `05e2603f86b9360a171fd81211f050885e487e5d`:

```console
$ /usr/bin/time -l bun run knip
[knip] 150/152 plugins/plugin-x: completed, 4 findings across 1 shards
[knip] 151/152 plugins/plugin-zai: completed, 12 findings across 1 shards
[knip] 152/152 plugins/plugin-zerollama: completed, 27 findings across 1 shards
[knip] 152 partitions, 2326 findings, completed
      631.30 real       839.43 user        56.83 sys
          1665597440  maximum resident set size
AFTER_EXIT_STATUS=0
```

```console
$ /usr/bin/time -l bun run knip:strict
[knip] 152 partitions, 2326 findings, failed
error: script "knip:strict" exited with code 1
      614.97 real       823.58 user        55.78 sys
          2610085888  maximum resident set size
STRICT_EXIT_STATUS=1
```

Peak RSS 1,665,597,440 bytes (~1.55 GiB) against a 16 GiB OOM. Advisory and strict produce the identical finding digest `cec20280350862f15937e41143c79f7b966d745e9937b9ead336fbb6d398d909`; strict differs only in exit code.

# Review responses

**@deepanshu-yd** — thank you for the read. Two points.

1. *"unblocks the CI verify gate"* — **this is not accurate, and I don't want it standing as a merge rationale.** `knip` is not part of `verify` and is not invoked by any workflow. At this head, `package.json#scripts.verify` is a chain of `check:*`, `audit:*`, `typecheck` and `lint:check` tasks with no `knip` in it, and `knip` appears in no file under `.github/workflows`. `bun run verify` exited 0 both before and after this change. What this PR unblocks is the **`knip` command itself**, which currently cannot return a result at all. Nothing about CI status changes.

2. *"1092 lines is substantial — verify partitioned results match monolithic for at least one workspace"* — conceded on both halves. The line count is real and it is the cost of keeping cross-workspace context rather than looping per package; I'd rather that be argued explicitly than hidden. On the equivalence check: a direct monolithic-vs-partitioned diff for a workspace **cannot be produced from the monolithic side**, because the monolithic run OOMs before emitting any report — that is the defect. What exists instead is `knip-partitioned.test.mjs:330`, which runs **real Knip** over a fixture repository and asserts that a cross-workspace consumer keeps its provider's export alive and that a nested package is owned exactly once. If you want a stronger equivalence signal, name a workspace small enough to run monolithically in isolation and I will post both reports side by side.

No `CHANGES_REQUESTED` review is outstanding on this PR; the GitHub reviews list is empty as of this writing.

# Deploy Notes

No deploy is included or authorized by this Draft PR.3a:T743,Relates to #24407
Epic: #20726

Stacked on #28786 at exact parent `01a44833fd67bc2537d5d5454e80ba6bd07eaceb`.

## What this does

- Atomically admits the next capture operation through the singleton backup operation lane.
- Binds capture authority to the exact live sandbox generation and immutable activation publication, including its exact node-history occurrence.
- Stamps the catalogue lease plus tenant and node fairness watermarks with one database-clock expiry.
- Supports exact replay and non-shortening renewal while fencing A/B/A caller-token cycles with `claimSequence`.
- Fails closed on sandbox, activation, token, funding, provider, node, or history drift and can skip a poisoned earlier catalogue row.
- Preserves the global lock order and intentionally does not row-lock the immutable activation publication.

## Scope boundaries

This split covers capture admission only. It does not wire the runtime worker, enable feature gates, create nodes, call providers, autoscale, deploy, or touch staging/production. Detached publication, verification, garbage collection, cohort/fairness scheduling, restore, and failover remain follow-up splits.

## Validation

- Admission PGlite: 11/11 tests, 72 assertions
- Global lock-order: 6/6 tests, 54 assertions
- Parent lane lifecycle: 12/12 tests, 109 assertions
- Parent migration: 5/5 tests, 19 assertions
- Cloud shared TypeScript: pass
- Targeted Biome: pass
- `git diff --check`: pass
- Exact-head adversarial review: CLEAN, no P0-P3 findings

Exact head: `22376786a933480fb4bf3b453dc11e62dc7c5996`

## Known limitation

PGlite validates transactional behavior in one embedded session; it is not evidence of real PostgreSQL multi-connection interleavings. That proof remains required before runtime activation.

# Risks

The composed API test restores its environment, mocks, spies, database state, and controlled gates. The PostgreSQL integration test uses an isolated disposable database, drains both contenders, closes its raw clients and pool, terminates residual sessions, drops the database, and restores environment values. It loudly skips unless an explicit real-PostgreSQL DSN is provided.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Adds two complementary container-admission regressions:

1. A composed PGlite/API test starts two concurrent POSTs for the same project. Both reach the real atomic repository admission method; both return 201 with the same durable container ID, while exactly one complete readiness/logout/pull/inspect/network/create/start transport sequence executes. A retained distinct-project case with limit one still proves one canonical 402 loser.
2. A real-PostgreSQL repository integration test opens independent sessions. A raw holder locks the organization row with `FOR UPDATE`; two repository calls block on two distinct backend PIDs. One case proves same-project single-flight converges to one row and one `created: true` / one `created: false`; the other proves distinct-project quota serialization yields one commit and one canonical `QuotaExceededError`.
3. The Docker-capable cloud `e2e-tests` lane invokes that PostgreSQL file with a fail-closed non-empty `APPS_TENANT_DB_TEST_DSN` guard, so the lock regression cannot silently self-skip in hosted CI.

Together the assertions pin:

- same-project convergence to one durable ID and one complete provider sequence, plus one exact distinct-project quota loser;
- real independent-session PostgreSQL lock contention at the repository boundary;
- response/project identity bound to the sole durable row;
- one readiness/logout/pull/inspect/network/create/start sequence in the composed API path;
- pending → building → deploying state and node allocation 0 → 1;
- billing snapshot `reserved=1`, `used=0`, `remaining=0`;
- bounded gate release, promise draining, and database cleanup on failures.

This is deliberately not evidence for a Workerd runtime, Hetzner Cloud, a Docker daemon, real SSH, or end-to-end PostgreSQL contention through the HTTP boundary.

## What kind of change is this?

Improvement: deterministic backend regression coverage only.

# Documentation changes needed?

No. The tests document their scope and exclusions inline; no public behavior or operator procedure changes.

# Risks

Medium — this adds the transaction authority used before future backup provider effects. No caller or provider mutation is wired in this Draft.

# What does this PR do?

Adds the exact lifecycle repository over the global backup-operation lane introduced by #28773.

- Claims one globally serialized lane with explicit `claimed`, exact-token `replayed`, and non-mutating `busy` outcomes.
- Requires an explicit primary-DB transaction and binds active proofs to its PostgreSQL XID, so a leaked transaction object cannot recreate authority after commit.
- Issues a monotone `bigint claimSequence` execution fence; A→B→A caller reuse gets a fresh sequence and stale executions remain rejected.
- Atomically stamps tenant and exact source-node occurrence watermarks with every fresh claim.
- Uses the primary DB clock for lease decisions and exact CAS predicates for renew/release.
- Re-locks and re-reads DB state when refreshing a proof; released, expired, superseded, foreign-transaction, or mutated proofs fail closed.
- Snapshots all caller inputs before the first asynchronous boundary and rejects malformed UTF-16, non-canonical UUIDs, invalid lease bounds, and invalid exact-node histories.

Reviewer entry points:

- `packages/cloud/shared/src/db/repositories/agent-backup-operation-lane.ts`
- `packages/cloud/shared/src/db/repositories/__tests__/agent-backup-operation-lane.pglite.test.ts`

- same suite repeated three times after removing a millisecond-boundary flaky: 12/12 each run;
- parent migration + global journal proof: 10/10 passed;
- Cloud shared TypeScript typecheck: passed;
- targeted Biome and `git diff --check`: passed;
- independent exact-head adversarial review: CLEAN P0-P3.

The lifecycle proof covers claim/replay/busy/reclaim, atomic rollback, tenant/node fairness receipts, explicit-transaction enforcement, XID-bound proof refresh, mutable-input TOCTOU, ABA fencing, renew/release exact CAS, primary-clock expiry, UTF-8/UTF-16 boundaries, canonical UUIDs, and exact node-history FKs.

## What this PR adds

`packages/agent/src/actions/plugin.ts` had no same-named test suite on
`develop`. This PR adds `packages/agent/src/actions/plugin.test.ts`, a new
+1192/-0 file — no existing line is touched.

The suite drives the real exported `pluginAction` through its public
`handler`/`validate` surface and asserts behavior a consumer observes, not
mocks:

- **Dispatch**: missing op, unknown op, `op` / `subaction` alias routing into
  the correct branch, and manager exceptions surfacing as typed
  `ActionResult`s instead of escaping.
- **Manager-service gate**: `validate` allowing/refusing manager ops based on
  the duck-typed `plugin_manager` service (including a non-conforming service
  failing the guard), and handler failures when the service is absent.
- **Package lifecycle via the injected manager seam**: install
  (`@elizaos/plugin-` normalization, whitespace trim, scoped-name passthrough,
  `connectorId` fallback, restart note, error propagation), uninstall (restart
  note variants), update (missing optional `updatePlugin`, release-stream
  forwarding, options omission), sync (raw un-normalized id — distinct from
  install), eject/reinject (result rendering plus the scheduled host restart,
  observed through `setRestartHandler` with fake timers).
- **Compat routes** (`configure` / `read_config` / `list` / `disconnect`) at
  the loopback HTTP boundary: exact request method/URL/body assertions,
  config value stringification, connection-test summary appending, error-text
  preference order, sensitive-value masking in text and data, exact-id-over-
  substring resolution, dedicated vs fallback disconnect endpoints, connector
  scoping by default type, search/configured/status filters, malformed-roster
  handling, and fetch-failure degradation.

The toggle verb was already pinned by `plugin-toggle.test.ts`; this suite does
not duplicate its cases.

41:T3a5d,A `user_sessions` row can stay `ended_at IS NULL` forever, and `/sessions/current` keeps counting it as the caller's *current* session. If a user closes the tab, if the Steward token simply expires, or if the best-effort logout write fails, the row survives — and `listActiveByUser()` / `getCurrentSessionStats()` still classify it as active purely because `ended_at IS NULL`. So the API reports lifetime usage from abandoned telemetry as current-session usage, and the IP / user-agent / device metadata collected for that request outlives the authentication event that justified collecting it.

`cleanupOldSessions()` cannot recover from this: it filters on non-null `ended_at`, so it can never select a stale null-ended row, and on `develop` no cron, route, or workflow calls it at all.

**Head this body describes: `833aa46a41a708ba8f6b66b810916b6f41e84042`.** Every claim below is about that head unless it is explicitly marked otherwise.

---
