## Response to @startrack3r's CHANGES_REQUESTED (posted on this exact head)

### 1. The backfill CLI's production gate is not bound to the database it mutates — conceded, and I am dropping the script

Correct, and the review understated nothing. I re-read `packages/cloud/shared/scripts/backfill-user-session-lifecycle.ts` at this head: `--environment` is parsed, validated against `"staging" | "production"`, and then used for exactly one thing — it is echoed into the aggregate JSON on stdout. The database handle comes from `import { ... } from "../src/db/client"`, which resolves from the process environment. Nothing correlates the two. An operator with `DATABASE_URL` pointed at production can pass `--environment=staging --apply` and never reach the `--staging-proof` branch, which is the only production-only guard. And that proof is accepted as any non-empty string. There is no test on this boundary.

I am not going to paper over an operator-safety hole with a stricter flag parser inside the same PR that is already carrying the schema change. **Recommendation, which I intend to push as the next commit on this branch: remove `packages/cloud/shared/scripts/backfill-user-session-lifecycle.ts` and its `db:backfill-user-session-lifecycle` entry in `packages/cloud/shared/package.json` from this PR entirely, and remove the backfill invocation blocks from `packages/cloud/shared/docs/user-session-telemetry-lifecycle.md`.** That is not done at `833aa46a`; the script is still present in the diff you are reading.

What I propose to keep is the repository layer it wrapped — `previewLifecycleBackfill()` and `applyLifecycleBackfillBatch()` — which are bounded, take no environment argument, print nothing, and are exercised by the PGlite test. The unsafe surface is the CLI that decides *which database* to point them at, and that surface belongs in a separate, reviewed operator-tooling PR with a trusted database-identity check and a test proving a production target cannot be applied under a staging label. I would rather ship this PR without any operator entrypoint than ship one whose only guard can be bypassed by an environment variable.

### 2. The `idle` closure reason is unreachable on the production auth path — conceded

Also correct, and I verified the arithmetic in-tree rather than taking it on faith:

- `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/auth/steward-client.ts` defines `export const STEWARD_ACCESS_TOKEN_TTL_SECONDS = 60 * 60;` and its comment states Steward mints access tokens at exactly that TTL.
- `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/auth.ts` decodes the verified token's `exp` and writes it to `token_expires_at`, and the same tracking path advances `last_activity_at` to request time.
- `packages/cloud/shared/src/db/repositories/user-sessions.ts` sets `USER_SESSION_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS = 60 * 60 * 1_000`, and the cleanup CTE classifies with

  ```sql
  CASE
    WHEN COALESCE(token_expires_at, started_at + interval '1 hour') <= now THEN 'expired'
    ELSE 'idle'
  END
  ```

Because `last_activity_at >= ` token issuance, a production row always satisfies `token_expires_at <= last_activity_at + 1 hour`. By the moment the row crosses the idle cutoff, the expiry branch is already true and it wins the `CASE`. `'idle'` is therefore dead for any row the real auth path created.

And the reviewer is right about the fixture: the idle row in `user-sessions.lifecycle.test.ts` is inserted with `last_activity_at = '2026-08-25 10:00:00'` and `token_expires_at = '2026-08-25 12:30:00'` under a frozen clock of `12:00:00` — a 2.5-hour-plus remaining token lifetime that Steward's 1-hour TTL cannot mint. The test proves the SQL branch executes; it does not prove the abandoned-session path. I withdraw that as evidence.

I do not want to guess the right resolution in a review reply, because there are two materially different ones and they have different policy consequences: either the idle window becomes shorter than the token TTL (so idle can actually win, and the abandoned tab is closed sooner than expiry would close it), or `idle` is dropped as a distinct reason and abandonment folds into `expired`. I would like a maintainer's read on which contract the cloud team wants before I re-cut the classification, and I will re-fixture with a token whose lifetime is exactly `STEWARD_ACCESS_TOKEN_TTL_SECONDS` so the case is production-realistic either way. Until that lands, treat the `idle` reason in this diff as unproven.

### 3. The provider-backed staging acceptance gate is unmet — conceded, and the `Closes` is now a `Refs`

Fair, and this was the sharpest of the three: leaving two acceptance boxes unchecked while declaring `Closes #28584` is a contradiction inside my own text. I have changed it to `Refs #28584` and moved the unmet criteria into an explicit "Remaining acceptance work" section below, so this PR is not presented as closure-ready. PGlite fixtures and route mocks do not prove the cloud identity/schema boundary the issue asks for, and I do not have authorization to mutate a staging account, database, or OAuth session from this environment.

## What this PR actually delivers

Scoped to what the diff supports, at this head:

- `packages/cloud/shared/docs/user-session-telemetry-lifecycle.md` (new) documents `user_sessions` as usage telemetry and names Steward's signed access cookie, Steward verification, Cloud account state, and the inference revocation fences as the authentication authority. A telemetry row is never authority.
- `0313_user_session_telemetry_lifecycle.sql` (additive) plus `packages/cloud/shared/src/db/schemas/user-sessions.ts` add `token_expires_at`, `ended_reason`, `retention_expires_at`, `metadata_purged_at`. No column is dropped or rewritten in place.
- `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/auth.ts` records the verified Steward token's absolute expiry on the existing best-effort tracking path. Tracking stays non-blocking and still cannot change an authentication result.
- Active reads (`listActiveByUser`, `getCurrentSessionStats`, `/sessions/current`) now require both an open token lifetime and an open one-hour idle window, not just `ended_at IS NULL`. Legacy rows with no recorded expiry fall back to `started_at + interval '1 hour'`, which can only shorten authority, never extend it.
- `packages/cloud/api/cron/cleanup-user-sessions/route.ts` (new) gives `cleanupOldSessions` a scheduled caller. The cleanup closes stale null-ended rows and deletes ended rows past their 30-day retention deadline, bounded at 500 rows per phase per invocation, using `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` and null-ended/ended-only predicates so retries and overlapping runs are safe.
- Closure immediately replaces the token locator with the non-secret `closed:<row-id>` and nulls IP, user agent, and device JSON.
- Cleanup emits only `scanned`, `closed`, `retained`, `deleted`, and duration — no row IDs, user or organization IDs, token hashes, or network metadata.

Not delivered: the `idle` classification (finding 2), a safe operator backfill entrypoint (finding 1), and any provider-backed staging evidence (finding 3).

## Refs

Refs #28584. This is deliberately not a `Closes`.

### Remaining acceptance work on #28584

These criteria from the issue are **not** met by this PR and stay open:

- [ ] **Backfill existing rows through a bounded staging-first migration with dry-run counts and rollback strategy.** The repository-layer preview/apply batching exists and is tested, but the operator CLI is being withdrawn (finding 1) and no staging row was changed. This needs a separate operator-tooling PR.
- [ ] **Provider-backed staging evidence** that a fresh session appears, activity updates it, logout/expiry removes it from active aggregates, and later retention deletes it. Interactive identity.slop.cash OAuth cannot be finalized from an unattended environment and I hold no authorization to mutate a staging account or database.
- [ ] **Record lifecycle reason … to distinguish … idle closure** — partially met. `logout`, `revoked`, `expired`, `administrative_cleanup`, and `legacy_ended` are recorded and tested; `idle` is written by the SQL but is unreachable on the production auth path (finding 2).

The remaining criteria — telemetry/authority documentation, an expiry or bounded-idle semantic that does not trust client logout, active-read exclusion, a scheduled idempotent cleanup over both ended and stale null-ended rows, metadata minimization and retention definition, retry-safety, and redacted aggregate metrics — are what this diff addresses.

## CI status on this head — currently red, and I am not claiming otherwise

`All Tests Passed` is **failing** on `833aa46a41a708ba8f6b66b810916b6f41e84042`. Observed via the API, not inferred:

- Run [32862455285](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/32862455285) (`.github/workflows/pr-static-smoke.yml`), head `833aa46a…`.
- `All Tests Passed` → `failure`. Its only failing step is `Require source and Windows security lanes`, whose log reads `RESULTS: source-smoke=cancelled browser-bridge-windows-security=success` then `##[error]source-smoke finished with cancelled`.
- `Browser bridge Windows security / windows-security-contract` → `success`.
- `Source static smoke` → `cancelled`. It started `14:54:35Z` and ended `15:24:54Z` — 30m19s against the workflow's `timeout-minutes: 30`. Every step through `Typecheck affected workspace closure` succeeded; the cancellation landed inside `Build affected workspace closure`, and the job's teardown log shows it terminating `cmake` / `gmake` / `cc1plus` processes under `plugins/plugin-local-inference/native/llama.cpp`.

So the red is a wall-clock timeout in a native submodule build, not a failing assertion from this diff — but it is red on the exact head, an approval on it would sit on a red gate, and I am not asking anyone to read "cancelled" as "green". This needs a re-run, and the re-push that drops the backfill script (finding 1) will produce a fresh head and a fresh run. If the timeout is reproducible on this branch rather than flaky, that is a separate problem I will report rather than retry past.

This branch is also `behind_by: 6` against `develop` (merge base `f2ccf2555d18d9ec768c3ace447f595799c44ed0`). I will rebase onto current `develop` with the same re-push, so the next head is not a stale base.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Adds one atomic read-only snapshot for the durable managed marker, registration authority, and TTL activation route.

The route carries the complete nested V1 endpoint envelope, including the canonical `runtimeAgentId`. The reader validates exact shape, generation, immutable server name, canonical runtime UUID, strict ASCII DNS/IPv4 HTTP endpoints with ports 1..65535, canonical SHA-256 content digest, and equality with the durable authority's generation/publication/hash. The runtime identity is content-addressed but is deliberately not compared with the managed sandbox ID; that binding belongs to the consumer in #28656.

The transport contract remains purpose-bound:

- Redis OSS adapters must use `EVAL_RO`.
- Upstash adapters must use `evalRo` with the byte-zero `no-writes,allow-key-locking` shebang.
- The authority reader is not handed a generic mutating `EVAL` capability.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix and non-breaking routing foundation.

## What changed

- elects and persists one completion coordinator while preserving legacy single-session and multi-attempt tasks
- records contributor completion without advancing the aggregate coding task
- requires a coordinator review turn after all required contributor receipts are delivered
- serializes completion-gate evaluation per coding task to prevent sibling races
- carries coordinator/contributor lineage through parent-broker nested spawns
- substitutes barrier membership for router-created state-lost and account-failover successors via `retryOfSessionId`
- transfers authority on resume, auto-spawn, new-session retry, rerun, and restart; superseded attempts remain non-required history
- persists review delivery success/failure, surfaces failures as `waiting_on_user`, and supports live/startup retry
- exposes additive typed authority/review fields in task DTOs

## Acceptance coverage

The real task service + durable in-memory store cover:

- contributor completes first
- coordinator completes while a required contributor is busy
- final contributor triggers a complete receipt-review prompt
- a second coordinator completion is required for aggregate validation
- simultaneous sibling completions send exactly one review
- production-like ACP review turn emits the authorizing completion
- blocked required contributor cannot settle the coding task
- delivery failure is durable/user-visible and retries without restart
- pending receipt delivery recovers after restart
- explicit authority transfer from a terminal coordinator
- legacy multi-attempt compatibility
- automatic coordinator state-lost successor handoff
- automatic required-contributor failover handoff
- legacy single-session compatibility
- parent broker propagates contributor role, parent session, required membership, and child session handle

# The defect

The canonical SQL membership authority fatally rejects the elizaOS platform's own ids. `packages/core/src/utils.ts:1143` deliberately forces the UUID version nibble to `0x0` for every `stringToUuid` / `createUniqueUuid` value, and core's own `uuidSchema` (`packages/core/src/utils.ts:1095-1100`) accepts that shape — but `plugins/plugin-sql/src/services/sql-membership.ts:52` mirrored the same idea as `[1-8]`, so `assertUuid` (same file, `:251-255`) throws `MEMBERSHIP_COMMAND_INVALID` with `severity: "fatal"` on **100% of them**. What a caller observes: an agent whose character has no explicit `id` gets `agentId = stringToUuid(character.name)` (`packages/core/src/runtime.ts:1577`), so *every* membership call it makes — `authorize`, snapshot apply, delta apply — dies at `assertUuid(scope.agentId, "agentId")` before touching the database. Connector-derived ids fail the same way: WhatsApp mints its world/room/entity ids through `createUniqueUuid` (`plugins/plugin-whatsapp/src/inbound-claim.ts:45,49,98`, `plugins/plugin-whatsapp/src/runtime-service.ts:287,732-752`), and those land on `assertUuid(member.canonicalPrincipalId | runtime.worldId | runtime.roomId | runtime.entityId, …)` at `sql-membership.ts:403-406`.

This is not a WhatsApp bug. It is a core-vs-plugin-sql contract split that makes the membership authority unusable for any connector that derives ids the documented elizaOS way. This PR is the first production caller that reaches it, which is how it was found.

# Reproduction: failing before, passing after

Deterministic, no database, no suite. The script reads the real `UUID_PATTERN` literal out of the repo file on disk and derives ids with the exact byte operations from `packages/core/src/utils.ts:1133-1145`. Save it as `/tmp/uuid-nibble-repro.mjs` and run both commands from the repo root:

```js
// /tmp/uuid-nibble-repro.mjs
import { readFileSync } from "node:fs";
import { createHash } from "node:crypto";

const src = readFileSync(process.argv[2] ?? "plugins/plugin-sql/src/services/sql-membership.ts", "utf8");
const literal = /const UUID_PATTERN = (\/.*\/i);/.exec(src)[1];
const UUID_PATTERN = new RegExp(literal.slice(1, -2), "i");
console.log("sql-membership UUID_PATTERN =", literal);

// packages/core/src/utils.ts:1133-1145, verbatim operations
function stringToUuid(target) {
  const bytes = createHash("sha1").update(encodeURIComponent(target)).digest().subarray(0, 16);
  bytes[8] = (bytes[8] & 0x3f) | 0x80;
  bytes[6] = (bytes[6] & 0x0f) | 0x00;              // version nibble forced to 0
  const h = Buffer.from(bytes).toString("hex");
  return `${h.slice(0,8)}-${h.slice(8,12)}-${h.slice(12,16)}-${h.slice(16,20)}-${h.slice(20)}`;
}
// packages/core/src/utils.ts:1095-1100
const coreUuidSchema = /^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}$/i;

const agentId   = stringToUuid("Eliza");                                              // runtime.ts:1577
const worldId   = stringToUuid(`whatsapp:inbound-claims:world:${agentId}`);            // createUniqueUuid
const principal = stringToUuid(`whatsapp-entity:120363000000000000@g.us:${agentId}`);  // createUniqueUuid

let rejected = 0;
for (const [name, id] of [["agentId", agentId], ["worldId", worldId], ["canonicalPrincipalId", principal]]) {
  const core = coreUuidSchema.test(id);
  const sql = UUID_PATTERN.test(id);
  if (core && !sql) rejected++;
  console.log(`${name.padEnd(21)} ${id}  core=${core}  sql=${sql}${core && !sql ? "  -> MEMBERSHIP_COMMAND_INVALID (fatal)" : ""}`);
}
console.log(`\n${rejected}/3 canonical ids accepted by core and fatally rejected by the SQL membership authority.`);
process.exit(rejected === 0 ? 0 : 1);
```

**Before** — against untouched `develop` (this PR's base, `74d659184ca0e85d1412b74beda88e52a63ae002`):

```bash
git show 74d659184ca0e85d1412b74beda88e52a63ae002:plugins/plugin-sql/src/services/sql-membership.ts > /tmp/before.ts
node /tmp/uuid-nibble-repro.mjs /tmp/before.ts; echo "exit=$?"
```

```text
sql-membership UUID_PATTERN = /^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[1-8][0-9a-f]{3}-[89ab][0-9a-f]{3}-[0-9a-f]{12}$/i
agentId               b850bc30-45f8-0041-a00a-83df46d8555d  core=true  sql=false  -> MEMBERSHIP_COMMAND_INVALID (fatal)
worldId               6fe80507-b7ee-0364-944c-e48c2e474dc4  core=true  sql=false  -> MEMBERSHIP_COMMAND_INVALID (fatal)
canonicalPrincipalId  2e010487-5d89-00a5-b89e-5c8cc1114666  core=true  sql=false  -> MEMBERSHIP_COMMAND_INVALID (fatal)

3/3 canonical ids accepted by core and fatally rejected by the SQL membership authority.
exit=1
```

**After** — against this PR's head, `77065f78eb51eccfa43e418898c7e1e3897da1ef`:

```bash
node /tmp/uuid-nibble-repro.mjs plugins/plugin-sql/src/services/sql-membership.ts; echo "exit=$?"
```

```text
sql-membership UUID_PATTERN = /^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-8][0-9a-f]{3}-[89ab][0-9a-f]{3}-[0-9a-f]{12}$/i
agentId               b850bc30-45f8-0041-a00a-83df46d8555d  core=true  sql=true
worldId               6fe80507-b7ee-0364-944c-e48c2e474dc4  core=true  sql=true
canonicalPrincipalId  2e010487-5d89-00a5-b89e-5c8cc1114666  core=true  sql=true

0/3 canonical ids accepted by core and fatally rejected by the SQL membership authority.
exit=0
```

Both outputs above were produced in this working session against the file contents at those two exact shas. The `3/3` is not a sample: `bytes[6] = (bytes[6] & 0x0f) | 0x00` makes the version nibble unconditionally `0`, so the old pattern rejected the full output range of `stringToUuid`.

## The whole fix

```diff
-const UUID_PATTERN = /^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[1-8][0-9a-f]{3}-[89ab][0-9a-f]{3}-[0-9a-f]{12}$/i;
+// Version zero is the deliberate deterministic format emitted by core stringToUuid/createUniqueUuid.
+const UUID_PATTERN = /^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-8][0-9a-f]{3}-[89ab][0-9a-f]{3}-[0-9a-f]{12}$/i;
```

One line, `+2 -1`. It widens the accepted version nibble from `[1-8]` to `[0-8]`; the variant nibble stays pinned to `[89ab]`, so it is still strictly narrower than core's `validateUuid`.

## Why existing coverage missed it

`plugins/plugin-sql/src/__tests__/integration/membership-authority.real.test.ts` on `develop` has 15 `it(` blocks and builds **every** id with `crypto.randomUUID()` (4 call sites, 0 uses of `stringToUuid` or `createUniqueUuid` in the file — verified by reading the file at base sha `74d6591`). `crypto.randomUUID()` emits v4, whose version nibble is `4`, which the old `[1-8]` pattern happily accepts. The authority was therefore fully green against ids that production never produces. The new integration test in this PR builds its principal ids with `createUniqueUuid` (`plugins/plugin-whatsapp/src/membership-publisher.pglite.integration.test.ts:6,64`) and asserts the connector account id's version-zero shape at `:73-75`, so the regression is now covered by a real-database test rather than by the pattern's own text.

# What else this PR ships

The one-line SQL fix is the blocker; the rest is the feature #25230 asks for, which is what surfaced it.

`plugins/plugin-whatsapp/src/membership-publisher.ts` (new, 609 lines) forwards Baileys group rosters and participant events into the canonical `MembershipService` through an account-local publisher wired at `plugins/plugin-whatsapp/src/baileys/connection.ts`, `src/clients/baileys-client.ts`, and `src/runtime-service.ts`. Reconnects publish complete rosters; a group absent from a *successful complete* reconnect snapshot becomes an authoritative empty roster; role changes re-query complete metadata before committing; explicit removals revoke immediately. Disconnect, failure, and source termination change scope *health* (`unavailable` / `stale`) without deleting known membership facts — absence is never inferred from partial, failed, stale, paginated-incomplete, or disconnected state. Cloud webhook senders stay observational: publishers are constructed only for a live `BaileysClient`, and no Cloud API roster or point-query claim was added.

Scope of `Closes #25230`, stated plainly: the issue says "explicit leave/kick/ban revokes immediately". Baileys exposes `add`, `remove`, `promote`, `demote`, `modify` — there is no distinct `ban` participant action. A provider ban arrives as `remove` and is revoked immediately, and non-self removals are recorded as `kicked`. If a maintainer wants a distinct provider-level `ban` reason recorded, that is not deliverable against Baileys today and I will drop the `Closes` to a `Relates to`.

# Overlapping work, named up front

**#28715** (`fix(telegram): wire membership authority admission and revocation into the Telegram connector`, opened 25 Aug 17:56 UTC, ~10h after this PR) independently hit the same defect and edits the same line, `plugins/plugin-sql/src/services/sql-membership.ts:52`:

```diff
-const UUID_PATTERN = /^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[1-8][0-9a-f]{3}-[89ab][0-9a-f]{3}-[0-9a-f]{12}$/i;
+const UUID_PATTERN = /^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}$/i;
```

Two things follow, and I would rather say them than be caught on them:

1. This is independent confirmation of the defect from a second connector, which is the strongest argument that the `[1-8]` pattern is wrong rather than that WhatsApp is special.
2. It is a textual conflict on one line. Their variant is the superset — full core `validateUuid` parity; mine keeps the variant nibble pinned to `[89ab]` and the version to `[0-8]`, which is deliberately conservative but is *not* core parity (a v9+/nonstandard id would pass core and still fail SQL). **If a maintainer prefers core parity, I will take #28715's wider pattern here — the conflict is one line and I will resolve it in whichever direction lands first.** No membership-authority behavior in this PR depends on the difference.

No other open or closed PR references #25230, and no other open PR touches WhatsApp membership (searched via the GitHub search API at the time of writing).

# Risks

Medium, concentrated in the new publisher rather than the SQL fix.

- The SQL change only widens an input-validation pattern; it grants no new capability and cannot admit a member the authority would otherwise deny, because admission is decided by the membership rows, not by the id's version nibble.
- The publisher introduces an authorization *evidence producer* on the Baileys socket lifecycle. The real risks are stale socket events, incorrect absence inference, and cross-account leakage. These are addressed by generation fencing, complete-snapshot-only semantics, explicit `unavailable`/`stale` health rather than deletion, fresh point queries before role commits, serialized mutations, and the account-isolation scenario in the real-PGlite test.
- Cloud API behavior is unchanged.

# Acceptance criteria

- [x] Publish complete Baileys group metadata snapshots to the canonical `MembershipService` — real-PGlite scenario 1.
- [x] Publish participant add/remove/promote/demote deltas — same scenario, all four actions including admin role changes and revocation.
- [x] Scope evidence by account + mode + external world + room — the account-isolation scenario proves two connector accounts cannot authorize each other's identical room/principal, and asserts the `baileys:` world identity.
- [x] Keep Cloud webhook senders observational only — publishers are constructed only for a live `BaileysClient`; no Cloud roster or point-query claim added.
- [x] Never infer removal from partial, failed, stale, paginated-incomplete, or disconnected state — the reconnect scenario keeps the roster active while health goes unavailable/stale.
- [~] Revoke explicit leave/kick/ban immediately — leave and kick are covered and revoke immediately; **a distinct provider `ban` reason is not recorded**, because Baileys has no `ban` participant action. See the scope note above. Ticking this box outright would contradict the paragraph two screens up, so it is not ticked.
- [x] Cover reconnect gaps and out-of-order delivery — reconnect repairs a missed removal; a stale `add` cannot resurrect a revoked member because current metadata is queried first.
- [x] Cover complete empty snapshots and source termination — both scenarios pass; termination fails closed without fabricating removals and ignores later events.

# Risks

Medium — this introduces a shared resolver but does not yet switch a production consumer to it.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Adds a fail-closed resolver that keeps the managed sandbox identity and runtime/character identity distinct.

- Managed authority snapshots are keyed only by `managedAgentId`.
- A ready managed endpoint must carry the exact requested `runtimeAgentId`; even a fully coherent and content-addressed route for another runtime is rejected before heartbeat and without legacy fallback.
- Managed routing reads only `server:<endpoint.serverName>:url` and requires the raw heartbeat URL to equal the `registryUrl` inside the content-addressed endpoint envelope.
- A second exact managed snapshot after the non-atomic heartbeat GET rejects revoke/republish races.
- Legacy fallback is allowed only when all managed authority keys are absent, then re-reads the legacy pointer and takes a final managed snapshot before returning.
- Any cutover or pointer change becomes `routing_state_changed`, never a stale route.
- Managed starting/revoked/conflict/unavailable states never consult legacy keys.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix and routing consumer foundation.

- `packages/cloud/services/_common/tests/agent-server-routing.test.ts`

- coherent cross-runtime endpoint rejected with zero heartbeat/legacy reads;

## Live failure addressed

The matched Flipt benchmark trajectory used a dense FILE object in which every optional schema field was materialized. The first glob placed the useful pattern in `glob`, left `pattern` empty, and placed a glob-like value in `path`, producing `missing_param: pattern is required`. Related calls used relative `.` and `internal/config` roots and failed absolute-path validation. Dense content-mode grep also carried a false line-number placeholder.

## Security review

Independent review identified and closed these boundary failures before publication:

- direct `../` and absolute glob traversal;
- encoded traversal such as `.[.]/outside/*.txt` and `{.[.],safe}/outside/*.txt`, where native `fs.glob` could read outside before candidate validation;
- directory symlink traversal and outside-target file symlinks;
- Bun/Node wildcard dotfile disagreement;
- minimatch default negation/comment controls for leading `!` and `#`.

Pattern text is now used only to match root-relative entries discovered by a contained `readdir(..., { withFileTypes: true })` walk. Directory symlinks are never descended, every matching leaf is sandbox-validated, and safe in-root file symlinks remain usable.

## What

Adds a new colocated unit suite, `packages/ui/src/gestures/useRafCoalescer.test.ts`, covering `useRafCoalescer` — the rAF-paced value coalescer that gesture surfaces use as the boundary between high-frequency pointer input and per-frame delivery. The module previously had no test coverage anywhere in the repository (`gestures.test.ts`, `momentum.test.ts` and `recognizers.test.ts` cover the neighboring math and recognizers, not this hook).

## Why

The hook makes several non-obvious guarantees its consumers depend on: at most one flush per animation frame, latest-value-wins coalescing, forced delivery on release (`flush`) without double delivery, and lossless final-value delivery even when `requestAnimationFrame` is unavailable. None of that behavior was pinned by any test.

## What the suite asserts (behavior, through the real hook)

- A burst of schedules collapses into exactly ONE sink call carrying the LATEST value, and nothing fans out before the frame paints.
- One rAF booking per burst; after the frame runs, the next schedule books a NEW frame (a stale pending marker would swallow the rest of the gesture).
- `flush()` delivers the pending value now, cancels the queued frame, and a late-fired frame cannot double-deliver.
- `flush()`/`cancel()` with an empty queue are silent no-ops that never touch `cancelAnimationFrame`.
- `cancel()` drops the pending value so neither the booked frame nor a later flush delivers it.
- Without `requestAnimationFrame`, delivery happens synchronously so the final value is never lost.
- A synchronous (inline-callback) rAF environment keeps delivering on every schedule — the pending-marker ordering documented in the source.
- Returned callbacks keep stable identity across renders while always invoking the LATEST sink.
- Unmounting mid-gesture cancels the in-flight frame so nothing delivers afterwards.

Frame timing is fully test-controlled via a manual rAF clock (`vi.stubGlobal`), mirroring the stub style of existing suites in this directory; every assertion drives the real module — no mocked subject.

## Evidence

- `bunx vitest run src/gestures/useRafCoalescer.test.ts` (packages/ui): **Test Files 1 passed (1), Tests 10 passed (10)** — reproduced against current `origin/develop` immediately before filing, both in the shared checkout and again at this branch's head in a clean worktree (1 file, 10/10).
- `bunx biome check src/gestures/useRafCoalescer.test.ts`: clean, "Checked 1 file… No fixes applied".
- `bunx tsc --noEmit` (packages/ui): the new file appears nowhere in the output; the pre-existing diagnostics are unchanged by this diff.

## What

Adds `packages/evidence/src/queue/index.test.ts`: a new unit suite for the GPU vision job queue's public barrel (`packages/evidence/src/queue/index.ts`), which previously had no same-named test file anywhere in the repo. The diff is one new file, +744/-0 — no production code changes.

## Why

The barrel declares the public surface of the queue module (#14543). Every sibling module had its own suite; the surface consumers actually import from was untested. This PR covers that boundary behaviorally.

## How it is tested

All runtime symbols are imported from `./index.ts` and driven for real — no module mocks:

- `parseJob`: valid round-trip incl. params; non-JSON -> typed `QueueJobInvalidError` (`QUEUE_JOB_INVALID`, issues preserved); empty-string field; array params; every missing field listed at once.
- `makeJobId` + `claimOrder`: timestamp-prefix FIFO ordering, same-ms tie determinism, non-job filtering + sort.
- `decideEnqueue`: accept below cap; refuse at/above cap with exact reason.
- Connectivity latch: healthy start; first-failure stamp without drain; drain exactly at window boundary and latching; full reset via `onServiceOk`.
- `isConnectivityFailure` across all four statuses; `drainSkipResult` honest-skip shape (no fabricated data).
- Typed errors: `QueueBackpressureError` name/code/message/context.
- `FileJobQueue` over real tmp directories: creates all four `QUEUE_DIRS`; enqueue -> oldest-first claim -> complete -> `readResult`; backpressure throw at cap; null result probe.
- `mergeAnalyzerResult` on real fs: fresh schema-1 doc creation; second analyzer preserves the first (no lost update).
- `processJob`: unknown-analyzer failure; completed contact merging data and clearing a latch; transient connectivity requeue leaving the job pending with no record; drained honest skip recording reason only.
- `runQueueWorker` with `stopWhenIdle`: drains to idle with correct per-action counts and claimed/processed/idle event sequence.
- `QueueExecutor`: cpu-tier delegation runs inline unchanged; gpu-tier with no worker produces the deterministic honest skip naming the missing worker while the job stays enqueued.
- `runQueueCli` over captured io: usage exit 0; structured `CLI_USAGE` error line + exit 1; enqueue writes one pending job and reports its id.

## What

Adds `packages/agent/src/runtime/operations/index.test.ts` — unit coverage for the RuntimeOperation public-surface barrel (`packages/agent/src/runtime/operations/index.ts`), which had no same-named test file on `develop`.

## Why

The barrel is the documented import surface every consumer of runtime operations goes through, but nothing pinned it. A broken or accidentally shadowed re-export would surface only at consumer call sites. The sibling implementation modules (`classifier`, `cold-strategy`, `health`, `health-checks`, `manager`, `reload-hot`, `repository`, `vault-bridge`) each have their own suites; this closes the remaining gap at the barrel itself.

## What the suite covers (15 cases)

- **Namespace surface:** `Object.keys` of the barrel namespace equals exactly the 24 documented runtime exports — sibling-internal helpers (`describeError`, `VaultResolveError`, `resolveOptimizedPromptIntegrityKey`) stay out of the public surface.
- **Re-export identity:** every binding is the same reference as its sibling module's direct export (`toBe`) across all eight source modules — proves no rewrap/shadowing.
- **Live behavior through the barrel (real modules, no mocks):**
  - classifier tiers via the barrel: restart → cold, same-provider switch → hot, `env.` config-reload → hot, non-hot path → cold;
  - `defaultClassifier` parity with `classifyOperation`;
  - end-to-end cold restart through `createColdStrategy`: replacement runtime returned, restart reason forwarded, single succeeded `cold-restart` phase with timestamps;
  - vault-ref round trip (`formatVaultRef` / `isVaultRef` / `parseVaultRef`, malformed inputs) and `vaultKeyForProviderApiKey` canonical output plus its two `TypeError` branches;
  - `builtInHealthChecks` contains exactly the four exported checks in order, each asserted with observed name/required/timeoutMs metadata;
  - `new HealthChecker()` with an empty registry reports `{ passed: [], failed: [], ok: true }`.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Consolidates five duplicate test suite pairs in `@elizaos/core`:
1. `src/build-variant.test.ts`: Retained colocated suite as canonical, merged caching test (5 original + 1 migrated = 6 tests); deleted `src/__tests__/build-variant.test.ts`.
2. `src/app-registry.test.ts`: Retained colocated suite, merged defensive copy, mutation isolation, and slug replacement assertions (3 original + 2 migrated = 5 tests); deleted `src/__tests__/app-registry.test.ts`.
3. `src/security/outbound-envelope-guard.test.ts`: Retained canonical colocated suite with envelope stream safety, merged `reportOutboundEnvelopeBlock` bounded preview test (10 original + 1 migrated = 11 tests); deleted `src/security/__tests__/outbound-envelope-guard.test.ts`.
4. `src/messaging/interactions/callback.test.ts`: Retained canonical colocated suite, added precise 60/61 byte boundary and 15/16 emoji unicode byte measurement assertions (11 original + 7 migrated = 18 tests); deleted `src/messaging/interactions/__tests__/callback.test.ts`.
5. `src/services/channel-topics.test.ts`: Retained canonical colocated suite, merged full LRU, hydration, persistence, and `matchTopicRooms` cross-channel search tests (3 original + 19 migrated = 20 tests, 60 total); deleted `src/services/__tests__/channel-topics.test.ts`.

**Regression detected:** Redundant duplicate suites that split test coverage, increase CI execution latency, and create desynchronization risk across `@elizaos/core`.
**Consumer who observes it:** CI pipeline and contributors maintaining build variant, app registry, security guards, callback codec, and channel topics.
**Why no higher-level test owns this:** The unit suites directly test pure variant resolution, registry singleton, envelope guard, codec byte measurement, and LRU/persistence algorithms; existing higher-level integration tests do not exhaustively exercise each granular boundary condition.

## What

Adds a new unit suite for the fog voice-orb concept (`packages/ui/stories/src/concepts/fog.ts`), placed with the package's collected tests at `packages/ui/src/__tests__/stories-concepts-fog.test.ts`. Test-only: no production source, config, or export changes — exactly one new file, +636/−0.

## Why

`fog.ts` had no same-named test anywhere on develop. The concept is consumed through `stories/src/concepts/index.ts`, which registers it in the CONCEPTS list the voice-orb gallery iterates, so every shell/mote behaviour the gallery relies on was unverified. The suite drives the real module through the same injected three/webgpu module surface the orb-kit harness hands every concept builder, and every expectation is recomputed independently — no mocked return value is asserted against itself.

## Coverage (21 cases)

- Descriptor contract: id/label `fog`, family `mood`, callable builder.
- Scene structure: ten sphere shells (24×16 segments, radii strictly descending within [0.7, 1.25]) followed by one 80-mote Points cloud with `frustumCulled = false`.
- Material contract: translucent, `depthWrite = false`, NormalBlending; slate-blue channel ordering (b > g > r) on every shell and on the mote cloud; build-time opacities 0.4 (motes) and [0.1, 0.15] (shells); mote size 0.022; initial mote radii within [0.5, 1.25].
- Frame animation: exact rest at t=0 (all rotations zero, unity scale); breathe scale `1 + energy·0.06` applied as a uniform scalar; respond colour lift of +0.05/+0.06/+0.08 per channel and opacity lift +0.015 held under the 0.24 ceiling; listen dim −0.008 stacking subtractively with respond via the same `min(0.24, …)` formula; each shell spins at a constant signed rate about exactly one axis (population {x:3, y:5, z:2}) with directions alternating across the stack while non-spinning components stay inside their ≤0.08 wobble bounds; shell centres wander on bounded ±0.025 sinusoidal drift on all three axes; every frame rewrites all 80 mote positions inside r ∈ [0.42, 1.33], actually moves them between distant frames, and flags the position attribute `needsUpdate`; mote opacity resets from the build-time 0.4 to the animated 0.34 floor (`0.34 + respond·0.12`).
- Input contract: the `low` band is provably unread (identical whole-state snapshot with low 0 vs 1); replaying any frame reproduces byte-identical world state (stateless per-frame math).
- Teardown: dispose releases all 11 geometries and all 11 materials and detaches everything the builder added.

## Summary

- capture validated host GOPATH, GOMODCACHE, and GOCACHE alongside executable-search authority, deriving platform-safe defaults from the OS account when Go relies on HOME
- preserve HOME sanitization and rebuild Go execution values only from the boot-captured parent baseline
- close ACP cold/warm trust boundaries by denying caller Go values and internal mirrors, stripping mirrors from warm claims, and rejecting mirror claims in the child
- preserve existing runtime-loader and Git-config spawn filtering

## Security boundary

Caller spawn env and custom credentials are untrusted. Raw Go cache variables, casing variants, and internal cross-module mirrors are denied at intake. Cold children receive parent-canonical values. Warm children receive canonical raw values through the authenticated one-shot claim, never internal mirrors; the child validates the claim before capturing its execution baseline. Missing cross-version mirror fields derive from the OS account home rather than mutable HOME or USERPROFILE.

## What

Adds a new unit suite for `DefaultRuntimeOperationManager` (`packages/agent/src/runtime/operations/manager.ts`), which had no test coverage on `develop`: the operations directory covered classifier, cold-strategy, health, health-checks, reload-hot and vault-bridge, but not the manager that sequences them.

## Why this file (target substitution)

This lane was assigned `packages/agent/src/actions/runtime.ts`, but open PR #27046 already adds `actions/runtime.test.ts` and modifies `runtime.ts` itself. Under one-PR-per-file this lane pivoted to the next uncovered module in the same package (`src/runtime/operations/manager.ts`), re-verified immediately before filing: no same-named suite on current `origin/develop`, zero open PRs referencing it, and `manager.ts`/`types.ts` byte-identical between the working tree base and `origin/develop`.

## Coverage (behaviour asserted, real class + real in-memory repository fixture — the subject is never mocked)

- Start outcomes: `accepted` (uuid id, pending status, empty phase log, tier persisted), `deduped` on a known idempotency key, `rejected-busy` carrying the active op id; idempotency gate checked before the busy gate.
- Classification: custom classifier tiers recorded; conservative `cold` default when none is wired; classify context snapshotted BEFORE `prepare()` runs — proven by mutating provider state inside prepare and asserting the classifier still sees pre-prepare values while a subsequent start sees the mutation.
- `prepare()` contract: returned intent replaces the request intent for both classification and execution; `undefined` keeps the original.
- Async lifecycle to success: validate → strategy-applied phases → health-check, with the strategy receiving the live runtime + prepared intent, its returned runtime passed to `runForRuntime`, phase details carrying passed/failed counts and monotone timestamps.
- Failure branches: `no-strategy-for-tier` (and its precedence over the runtime lookup), `no-runtime`, plain throw → `strategy-failed`, thrown `code: "vault-resolve-failed"` preserved, failed health check flipping only the last phase to `failed` while earlier phases stay immutable.
- Single-flight: concurrent submissions serialize; the second is rejected busy against the in-flight op id, then the first completes to succeeded.
- Query delegation: `get`/`list`/`findActive` passthrough.

## Evidence (test-only change; counts quoted from this exact tree)

- `bunx vitest run src/runtime/operations/manager.test.ts` (in `packages/agent`, vitest-owned lane): **Test Files 1 passed (1); Tests 18 passed (18)** — re-fetched and re-verified against current `origin/develop` immediately before filing.
- `bunx biome check src/runtime/operations/manager.test.ts`: clean, 0 problems.
- `bunx tsc --noEmit` (packages/agent): grep for `manager.test.ts` returns nothing — the new file contributes zero type errors.
- Diff is strictly additive: 1 new test file, +601/−0. No production behaviour changed; hosted CI does not run on fork PRs, so the counts above are the verification record.

Note: the first draft of this suite asserted two behaviours the code does not have (pending visibility after an await, strict finishedAt > startedAt within the same millisecond). Both were corrected to observed behaviour before filing; every assertion now records what the manager actually does.

# Evidence Gate

Evidence must match the exact commit reviewed. `scripts/pr-evidence.mjs rows`
sets this marker from the live PR head; rerun it after every push.
<!-- evidence-head:322ddabdf32553c7be55ef1ead82ff6dcd76c54a -->

Any change testable on the frontend is not mergeable without a video walkthrough,
before/after screenshots, and logs. If you did not attach them, say why.

Attach each applicable artifact **inline in this PR** (drag-and-drop into the
description or a comment), or write `N/A - <reason>` on the row. Do not leave
evidence rows blank. Videos must be **MP4** (GitHub renders them inline);
prefer **JPG over PNG** for screenshots. Do not commit evidence files to the
repo.

# Evidence Details

## Real LLM-call trajectory

For agent/action/provider/prompt/model changes, use a real live-model run, not
the deterministic proxy. Produce with:

```bash
  packages/scenario-runner/bin/eliza-scenarios run <scenario> --report <out.json>
```

Link the JSON report, run viewer, native jsonl, or write `N/A - <reason>`.

## Backend + frontend logs

Backend: structured logger lines ([ClassName] …) showing the code path firing end to end.
Frontend: console + network trace showing the request/response and state change.
Paste here inline (wrap long output in a `<details>` block), or write
`N/A - <reason>`.

## Screenshots (before / after) + video walkthrough

Full-page before AND after screenshots are required for any UI change. Include a
video click-through of the flow.

```bash
  bun run evidence:doctor                 (check capture tools; prints fixes for any missing)
  bun run test:e2e:record                 (general E2E recordings)
  bun run test:matrix:review              (capture into one verified evidence bundle)
  bun run evidence:review:no-open -- --bundle=evidence/runs/<run-id>
                                          (re-open the exact matrix run)
  bun run --cwd packages/app audit:app    (app + cloud UI — REQUIRED for UI changes)
```

The matrix command creates and verifies one named bundle, then passes that
exact run to the reviewer. Do not replace the `--bundle` path with raw producer
directories; `--source` is only for explicit archived/ad-hoc compatibility.

A UI-touching diff (rendered `.tsx`/`.css`/`.svg` under `packages/app`,
`packages/ui`, `apps/app`, …) MUST attach real screenshot/video/OCR artifacts —
the CI evidence gate rejects `N/A` on those rows for such diffs, label or not.
If a capture tool is missing, `evidence:doctor` prints the install command;
install it rather than marking evidence `N/A`.

For voice / transcript / TTS / STT / omnivoice changes, attach captured audio of
the real round-trip plus a narrated walkthrough. Or write `N/A - <reason>`.

## Known gaps / failures

List any command failure, missing artifact, unavailable device, unavailable live
service, or evidence row marked N/A. Include the exact reason and why it is not a
blocker for this PR.

## Summary

Adds the first unit suite for `packages/evidence/src/vision-qa/index.ts` — the public barrel every vision-qa consumer imports. Test-only change: one new file, `packages/evidence/src/vision-qa/index.test.ts`, **+600 / -0**, no production behavior touched.

The barrel previously had no same-named suite anywhere in the repo. Because each submodule already carries its own tests, these cases exercise the implementations *through* `./index.ts`, so a broken re-export, renamed export, or wiring regression now fails here even when submodule suites stay green.

## What is covered (37 cases)

- `renderQuestionPrompt` — per-question `- id "..."` bullet rendering in input order
- `parseAnswers` — happy path plus typed `VISION_RESPONSE_INVALID` rejections: non-JSON, missing asked id, unknown extra fields (strict shape), out-of-range confidence
- `AnthropicBackend` — Messages API URL/auth/version headers, rubric-as-system prompt, base64 image block, corrective retry turn appended as trailing block, multi-text-block join with real provider usage mapping, typed throw when no text content
- `OpenAiCompatibleBackend` — keyless local server omits `Authorization`, keyed sends `Bearer`, system rubric pinning, data-URI image part, corrective turn after the image block, usage field mapping (`prompt_tokens`→`inputTokens`), typed throw on null content
- `CliVisionBackend` — full claude protocol through the module's own injectable runner seam (argv shape, staged-image prompt, envelope parse to canonical usage), nonzero exit surfaced as typed `VISION_CLI_EXIT`
- `parseClaudeEnvelope` / `parseCodexUsage` — snake_case usage canonicalization, error-envelope refusal, no-usage refusal, JSONL noise skipping, last `turn.completed` wins, missing-event typed failure
- cache — canonical hash stability under question key reorder, dimension binding, `<root>/.vision-qa-cache/<sha>/<query>.json` layout round trip on a real tmp dir, corrupt-file-degrades-to-miss
- `scaleToMaxEdge` — no upscaling at/below cap, aspect-preserving downscale, zero-dimension clamp on extreme aspect ratios
- `suggestQuestions` — clean analysis yields nothing, blue-fraction threshold in both snake/camel conventions, diff-region question gated on bbox presence, OCR dev-string flags, unmet required copy and found forbidden copy
- `buildQaRecord` — projection into the schema-1 audit record

## Verification

Fork PR: hosted CI does not run on this repository's forks, so all counts below are from local runs against current `develop` (`5161753`, tree identical upstream except this test file).

- `bunx vitest run packages/evidence/src/vision-qa/index.test.ts` → **1 file passed, 37/37 tests passed**
- `bunx biome check packages/evidence/src/vision-qa/index.test.ts` → clean ("No fixes applied")
- `bunx tsc --noEmit` in `packages/evidence` → zero diagnostics mentioning `index.test.ts`

55:T2d4a,
## What this PR does

Adds a new unit suite for the `lava` voice-orb concept (`packages/ui/stories/src/concepts/lava.ts`), which had no same-named test file anywhere on develop. The diff is a single new test file: `packages/ui/src/__tests__/stories-concepts-lava.test.ts` (+573/-0). No production code is touched.

## How it is tested

The suite drives the real module through the seam orb-kit designs for exactly this purpose: concepts receive an injected `THREE` module surface (`WebGPUModule = Record<string, any>`), so the tests hand lava a recording double and assert lava's own computed behaviour — every expectation is recomputed in the test from observed state or source formulas, never read back from the double:

- descriptor contract (id/label/family, callable builder)
- scene assembly: glass shell + 7 blobs + 28-bubble points cloud; staged radius order; warmed crystal-glass shell override (`attenuationDistance` 2.5 vs kit default 3.0, amber attenuation colour) produced through the real `makeCrystalGlass` helper
- frame animation: shell-containment clamps (`|y| <= 0.85`, `hypot(x,z) <= 0.9 - radius/2`) held across sampled beats up to peak voice energy; swell band bounds; exact emissive heat mix `0.7 + energy*0.55 + respond*0.25`; respond-driven warmth shift with red-channel saturation clamp at 1 while blue stays fixed; bubble sine ride inside the 0.82 ring; exact opacity mix; fixed 0.0035 rad/frame shell yaw accumulation
- input-insensitivity to `listen`/`low`, and replay determinism of positions at a revisited instant
- dispose: all 9 geometries and all 9 materials disposed, parent emptied

## Evidence (test-only change)

- `bunx vitest run src/__tests__/stories-concepts-lava.test.ts` (in `packages/ui`): **Test Files 1 passed (1), Tests 17 passed (17)** — re-run against current origin/develop base immediately before filing.
- `bunx biome check --write src/__tests__/stories-concepts-lava.test.ts`: clean ("Checked 1 file ... No fixes applied" after the initial format pass).
- `bunx tsc --noEmit` in `packages/ui`: the new test file appears nowhere in the output (checked by grep in two environments; remaining diagnostics are pre-existing and unrelated to this file).
- The diff touches only one new test file (+573/-0).

Note: this is a fork contribution, so hosted CI does not run on this PR; the counts above are quoted from local runs.

## Screenshots (before / after) + video walkthrough

```bash
  bun run evidence:doctor                 (check capture tools; prints fixes for any missing)
  bun run test:e2e:record                 (general E2E recordings)
  bun run test:matrix:review              (capture into one verified evidence bundle)

The matrix command creates and verifies one named bundle, then passes that
exact run to the reviewer. Do not replace the `--bundle` path with raw producer
directories; `--source` is only for explicit archived/ad-hoc compatibility.

A UI-touching diff (rendered `.tsx`/`.css`/`.svg` under `packages/app`,
`packages/ui`, `apps/app`, …) MUST attach real screenshot/video/OCR artifacts —

56:Tef1,## Summary

Adds `plugins/plugin-computeruse/src/sessions/canonical-compat.test.ts` (558 lines, 31 tests) — the first direct coverage for the compat adapter introduced in #28389 (`canonical-compat.ts`, previously zero tests):

- **`toCanonicalInteractionSession`** — host/browser/sandbox/remote_guest target mapping (display / browser_tab / virtual_desktop surfaces), targetId-over-kind surface-id precedence, lease-expiry passthrough to `expiresAt`, contract version propagation.
- **`authorizeCompatibilitySessionAction`** — the legacy command table pinned by exact kind + payload observation at the real core authorization seam: click vs double_click vs context_click, signed scroll deltas for all four directions, modifier join order, `element_index:N` id form, drag path start/end + `startCoordinate`/x1,y1 fallbacks, `set_value` value-fallback, `browser_open` url-presence split, `browser_wait` timeout default, `launch`/`kill_app`/`open`, browser tab management, and unknown-verb / sandbox / remote_guest opaque-request preservation (`JSON.stringify({command, parameters})` verbatim).
- **Authorization outcomes** — stopped/paused sessions, expired host lease, action predating its session (typed `INVALID_INTERACTION_CONTRACT` / message assertions); the compat layer delegates the executable-state gate to the core authority rather than pre-filtering.
- **Browser target authority** — `browser_click` and `browser_navigate` dispatch through the real authority on a `managed_browser` / `browser_tab` session.

The core `authorizeInteractionDispatch` is spy-wrapped with the repo's established `importOriginal`-spread module-mock pattern, and the spy always delegates to the real implementation — every observed mapping must also pass real authorization; the module under test is never mocked.

57:T3025,
## What

Adds a new unit suite for the liquid-metal voice-orb concept at
`packages/ui/stories/src/concepts/liquidmetal.ts`, which had no same-named test
file anywhere on `develop`. The diff is one new file,
`packages/ui/src/__tests__/stories-concepts-liquidmetal.test.ts` (+549/-0); no
production code is touched.

## Why

The concept module is pure runtime behaviour with zero coverage: it assembles a
chrome sphere through the injected `three/webgpu` + `three/tsl` surfaces, retunes
the orb-kit gem material into a smooth mirror, wires a fresnel rim light through
the live `uAccent`/`uRespond` uniforms, and deforms 1,617 vertices every frame.
The suite follows the established `stories-concepts-*` convention (see
`stories-concepts-lava.test.ts`) and drives the real module through hand-rolled
in-memory doubles whose calls are recorded; every expectation is recomputed in
the test, never read back from the subject.

## Coverage

- Descriptor contract: `id: "liquidmetal"`, `label: "liquid"`,
  `family: "abstract"`, callable builder.
- Scene assembly: exactly one sphere mesh on the parent at radius 0.95 with
  48x32 segments (1,617 vertices), material retuned from the
  `makeChromeGem` defaults (`flatShading: false`, `roughness: 0.03`,
  `metalness: 1`, `envMapIntensity: 1.8`, colour `(0.95, 0.97, 1.0)`).
- Rim light: lazy-evaluated TSL stand-ins prove the emissive graph re-prices
  live with `uRespond` (exact `(0.08 + 0.55*respond)/0.08` factor) and scales
  linearly with `uAccent`.
- Frame animation, all recomputed independently:
  - displacement stays purely radial for every vertex across beats
    (tangential residual < 1e-5);
  - amplitude envelope holds at the declared worst case
    `0.12 * (0.3 + 0.9 + 0.35)` while the blob still moves at idle;
  - idle wobble scales exactly linearly with energy (per-vertex ratio
    `(0.3+0.45)/0.3 = 2.5`);
  - crest sharpening activates only above the respond threshold and pushes
    crests beyond pure amplitude gain without ever flipping phase;
  - the threshold boundary itself: `respond = 0.01` still tracks pure
    amplitude scaling (strict `>` branch);
  - position attribute marked dirty + normals recomputed once per frame;
  - yaw drifts linearly (`0.08 * t`) while pitch rides its bounded sine;
  - `listen`/`low` inputs provably change nothing (byte-identical snapshot);
  - replaying the same instant reproduces byte-identical positions.
- Teardown: geometry + material disposed, mesh removed from the parent.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

1. `src/build-variant.test.ts`: Retained canonical colocated suite, added variant resolution caching assertion across environment changes (6 tests); deleted `src/__tests__/build-variant.test.ts`.

4. `src/messaging/interactions/callback.test.ts`: Retained canonical colocated suite, added 60/61 byte-boundary checks and multi-byte 4-byte Unicode emoji measurement tests (12 tests); deleted `src/messaging/interactions/__tests__/callback.test.ts`.
5. `src/services/channel-topics.test.ts`: Retained canonical colocated suite containing all 19 full integration tests (LRU eviction, persistence, FIFO, concurrency serialization, hydration, error reporting) plus cross-channel topic search matching (20 tests); deleted `src/services/__tests__/channel-topics.test.ts`.

**Regression detected:** Redundant duplicate test executions across `packages/core` that slow down CI build matrices and create test divergence risks.
**Consumer who observes it:** CI test runner and developers maintaining build variants, curated apps, outbound envelope security, interaction callback codecs, and channel topics LRU service.
**Why no higher-level test owns this:** The unit suites directly test pure variant resolution caching, array copy mutation guards, streaming envelope latching, and low-level LRU eviction and concurrency serialization.

59:Ta79,## Summary

Closes the planner/evaluator context-locality portion of #24299.

- keeps each turn's rendered base context immutable and appends complete assistant/tool/evaluator/retry history instead of rewriting the second chat message on every iteration
- assigns stable room-scoped, stage-separated local cache identities (`<room>:planner` / `<room>:evaluator`) while preserving the shared provider prefix key
- preserves pending multi-tool calls as complete redacted append-only feedback before ordinary-chat evaluation, so `NEXT_RECOMMENDED` can execute the remaining call
- makes both local-inference text adapters prefer the complete chat-message ledger over duplicate prompt segments and losslessly serialize native tool-call/tool-result parts
- retains the evolving context event stream for trajectory observability; no truncation, compaction, slicing, or tool-result omission

## Consumer-visible failure fixed

Previously, evaluator/tool/retry events were appended into the context object and the entire context user message was re-rendered. That changed bytes before the existing assistant/tool suffix, destroying prefix-cache locality. The local-inference adapters compounded this by selecting `promptSegments`, which do not carry the assistant/tool history, whenever both representations were present.

A multi-tool ordinary-chat response also needs its still-queued calls in the evaluator input. The new ledger records the pending call IDs, names, complete non-secret arguments, and status before evaluation; the default-evaluator regression proves the recommended second call executes.

## Exact-head verification

Head: `12d13e83f1c81`
Base at verification: `78a866181f481`

- `bunx vitest run src/runtime/__tests__/planner-loop.test.ts src/runtime/__tests__/planner-loop-message-stacking.test.ts src/__tests__/planner-happy-path.test.ts` from `packages/core`: 257 passed

# Risks

Medium — this switches the Cloud workflow proxy from sequential legacy GETs to the managed activation authority and adds a read-only Lua capability to the shared Redis client.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Wires workflow proxy requests to the atomic managed/legacy routing resolver while preserving the existing tier and wake gates.

- Uses `agent_sandboxes.id` as the managed authority identity and `character_id` as the runtime identity.
- Proxies workflow paths with the validated runtime identity, never the managed sandbox ID.
- Prevents legacy fallback after any managed marker, including starting, revoked, conflict, mismatch, or unavailable authority states.
- Adds direct Redis `EVAL_RO` support with no fallback to mutating `EVAL`.
- Selects distinct direct-Redis and Upstash read-only scripts and fails closed for unknown transports.
- Reads legacy pointers and heartbeats through a prefixed presence envelope, preserving missing keys versus the literal value `"null"` despite Upstash JSON auto-deserialization.
- Builds the Redis client lazily, so invalid managed/runtime UUIDs are rejected before Redis is touched.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix and workflow routing consumer integration.

## What is covered

- `deriveEvmAddress` — golden vectors (including the well-known `0x…01` key), `0x`-prefix independence, determinism, and EIP-55 mixed-case output
- `validateEvmPrivateKey` — success, wrong-length, and invalid-hex branches, plus agreement with the derivation path
- `validateSolanaPrivateKey` / `deriveSolanaAddress` — 64-byte base58 secrets, 32-byte base58 seeds, JSON numeric byte arrays, non-numeric array rejection, wrong decoded length, base58 alphabet violations, and redaction placeholders
- `validatePrivateKey` — chain auto-detection routing (`0x` prefix and bare 64-hex to EVM, short hex and base58 to Solana)
- `maskSecret` — the 8/9-character masking boundary
- `generateWalletKeys` / `generateWalletForChain` — generated material survives the module's own validators back to the reported addresses
- `importWallet` / `setSolanaWalletEnv` — the `process.env` contract (trimming, `0x` normalization, public-key mirroring, untouched env on failure, including the write-before-sync-fails behavior of `setSolanaWalletEnv`)
- `getWalletAddresses` — full resolution-order matrix: steward > local > managed legacy fallback, cloud-over-managed selection, explicit-source suppression of fallback, unrecognized source values, and malformed address rejection
- `getWalletAddressesWithSteward` — offline degrade boundaries exercised against an unreachable loopback endpoint (no mocks): returns the base addresses rather than throwing
- `fetchSolanaNativeBalanceViaRpc` — empty/blank URL lists throw the RPC-unavailable sentinel, and multi-endpoint failures aggregate per-host errors in request order

Every expectation was recorded against this exact module (golden derivation vectors); no mocks stand between the tests and the module.

Adds the first unit coverage for `packages/ui/stories/src/concepts/ember.ts`, the ember voice-orb concept (charcoal faceted body, lava-crack wireframe, furnace core, orbit sparks). The suite drives the real exported `concept` through its full `build -> frame -> dispose` lifecycle over a minimal faithful WebGPU/TSL double — the shape orb-kit itself documents as fake-drivable (`WebGPUModule = Record<string, any>`, "lets the harness drive the orb from a fake") — and asserts observed behavior, never mock echoes:

- catalog contract: id/label/family and a callable builder
- wiring: exactly four children in order; crack wireframe wraps the SAME body geometry instance; sparks carry 90 seeded points and ember's recolor override of the kit's default bluish particle color; transparency stack (wire transparent + depthWrite off, core transparent)
- geometry: the kit's real facet perturbation executes through ember's chosen params (radius 1.0, lump 0.1) — vertex 0 lands at `fround(1 + (sin(5.2)*0.5 + sin(5.9)*0.2) * 0.1)` in the Float32 position buffer, and the per-vertex centroid bake writes three equal entries
- idle frame at t=0: heat 0.475 drives every derived color/emissive/opacity/scale value (pinned numerically)
- animation: rotations follow time and energy (body y=t*0.04, wobble x, cracks mirror, core counter-rotates, sparks orbit speed scales with energy); sustained energy clamps totalHeat at 1.0 and every heat-driven output pins at its ceiling
- respond flare state machine: strictly-greater-than-0.7 trigger (respond=0.7 exactly never fires), single-frame spike, linear decay back to the bit-exact idle baseline, and the `flareDecay < 0.1` hysteresis producing a sawtooth re-arm under sustained responding
- dispose releases all six geometries/materials plus the particles' own resources and empties the parent

Placement note: the suite lives in `packages/ui/src/__tests__/` rather than beside the source because the package vitest lane collects only `src/**`, `__tests__/**`, and `test/**`, and the package tsconfig includes only `src` — a suite inside `stories/src/` would never be collected by CI nor typechecked.

## What

Adds a new unit suite for `packages/ui/src/components/chat/message-maps-parser.ts` — the parser that turns `[MAPSCARD]` wire blocks emitted by the MAPS action family (`plugins/plugin-maps/src/card.ts`) into validated card specs consumed by the chat widget renderer (`widgets/maps-card.tsx`, `widgets/inline-builtins.tsx`). The module had no same-named test anywhere on `develop`.

The diff is a single new test file (+511/-0). No production code is touched.

## Coverage

Real behaviour of the real module — no mocks standing in for the subject:

- **`parseMapsCardBody`** — all five card kinds (`place`, `places`, `route`, `handoff`, `locate`) parsed end-to-end from JSON fixtures, plus rejection paths: malformed JSON, non-object payloads, unknown kinds; coordinate range boundaries (±90/±180 inclusive pass, beyond fails); string-length bounds (300-char name passes, 301 rejected); category filtering order (invalid entries dropped *before* the 8-cap); the `MAX_MAPS_CARD_PLACES` overflow rule (12 entries keep the first 10; trailing garbage after a full page is never parsed, garbage below the cap rejects the card); over-long optional fields degrading to `null` rather than rejecting (`attribution`, `nextCursor`); absent/empty `formattedAddress`/`sharedAt` omitting their keys entirely; route travel-mode allowlisting and metric bounds; handoff URI policy enforcement; locate intent validation.
- **`isAllowedMapsUri`** — `geo:` scheme requires a leading coordinate digit (negative coordinates pass, `+`-prefixed and non-numeric fail); https-only links restricted to `maps.apple.com` and `www.openstreetmap.org`; lookalike hosts, wrong schemes, and unparseable strings rejected.
- **`findMapsCardRegions`** — exact fence-to-fence offset contract (`text.slice(start, end)` reproduces the full block including fences); multiple cards collected in order with malformed blocks skipped mid-scan; padded fences and CRLF tolerated; no-card text returns `[]`; repeated calls do not leak global-regex `lastIndex` state between invocations.

## Scope

Test and CI wiring only. No production code, runtime gate, deployment, provider resource, staging, or production mutation is included.

## Deployment

None. This PR remains Draft.60:T2fd6,
## What

Adds a new unit suite for `packages/ui/stories/src/concepts/aurora.ts`, the aurora voice-orb concept (five curved translucent ribbons plus a drifting star cloud), which previously had no test coverage anywhere on develop.

**Placement note:** `stories/` has no test runner of its own (no package.json, and the package vitest lane collects only `src/**/*.test.*`), so the suite lives at `packages/ui/src/__tests__/stories-concepts-aurora.test.ts`, where the owning package's existing unit lane collects and typechecks it. The subject is imported directly from its canonical location.

## How it tests

The suite drives the **real** module through the same seam production uses: `concept.build(THREE, TSL, U, parent)` receives its three.js surface as an injected parameter (`WebGPUModule = Record<string, any>` per orb-kit), so a recording double supplies that boundary while every expectation is recomputed independently in the test — nothing is asserted by reading a mock's own output back.

Covered behaviour:

- **Descriptor contract** — id/label/family registration, callable builder.
- **Ribbon geometry bend** — five PlaneGeometries bent onto a cylindrical arc: equator vertices keep their flat positions, every off-equator vertex gains negative depth (backward wrap), ribbon ends flare past the flat plane width, the height grid stays symmetric about the equator, normals are computed.
- **Material contract** — each ribbon is a translucent, non-depth-writing, double-sided normal-blended curtain at opacity 0.45; base hues are pairwise distinct within unit range (per-ribbon hue preservation).
- **Star cloud** — exactly 60 points inside the documented r∈[0.5, 1.25] shell, small cool translucent points, frustum culling disabled.
- **Frame animation** — idle floor opacities (0.42 ribbons / 0.5 stars), response-curve brightening recomputed from captured bases with channels saturating at exactly 1, opacity clamping at the 0.7 ceiling, five pairwise-distinct headings while swaying, pitch/position envelope bounds across sampled times, fixed star drift rates, insensitivity to `listen`/`low` inputs, and whole-state determinism when a frame replays.
- **Dispose** — every geometry and material disposed, all six added children detached from the parent group.

No `expectTypeOf`, no `vi.mock` of the subject, no type-only assertions.

- Diff is exactly one new file, +508/−0; no existing test was modified or removed

## What

Adds `packages/shared/src/api/http-helpers.test.ts` — a new, additive-only suite (+492/−0, no other files touched) covering the runtime surface re-exported by `packages/shared/src/api/http-helpers.ts`: `DEFAULT_MAX_BODY_BYTES`, `readRequestBodyBuffer`, `readRequestBody`, `readJsonBody`, `sendJson`, and `sendJsonError`.

Every case drives the real exported helpers against a live `node:http` exchange on loopback — no mocks of the module under test. The covered behavior:

- **Bounded body reads** — chunk concatenation; repeat readers receive the SAME memoized buffer (`Symbol.for` cache) without re-consuming the stream; empty bodies; explicit `encoding` handling (utf-8/base64).
- **Size guards** — default cap rejects oversized bodies with typed `HTTP_REQUEST_BODY_TOO_LARGE` carrying `{maxBytes, observedBytes}` context; custom `tooLargeMessage`; `returnNullOnTooLarge` / `destroyOnTooLarge` combinations including stream destruction; the guard re-applied to an already-memoized buffer on repeat reads.
- **`readJsonBody`** — object parsing memoized on the request (`req.body` exposed, second call served from cache); non-object rejection by default (arrays/scalars/null) with 400 JSON error envelope; `requireObject: false` accepts arrays and scalars; dedicated parse-error vs non-object statuses/messages, all overridable; read failures (including size overflow) translated to the configured read-error response (default 413).
- **Safe responders** — `sendJson` / `sendJsonError` status defaults and overrides, `application/json` content type, `{error}` envelope, and no synchronous throw when the client vanished before the write.

Note: an existing suite covers core's own `packages/core/src/api/http-helpers.ts` implementation directly; this PR covers the shared re-export surface that shared-package consumers actually import.

## Evidence

Test-only change; no production behavior modified.

- `bunx vitest run packages/shared/src/api/http-helpers.test.ts` → **Test Files 1 passed (1); Tests 19 passed (19)** (also green via the package lane `node ../scripts/run-vitest.mjs run --config ./vitest.config.ts src/api/http-helpers.test.ts`).
- `bunx @biomejs/biome check src/api/http-helpers.test.ts` → clean, no fixes needed.
- `bunx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` in `packages/shared` → the new test file appears nowhere in the output.

  bun run evidence:review:no-open -- --bundle=evidence/runs/<run-id>
                                          (re-open the exact matrix run)
  bun run --cwd packages/app audit:app    (app + cloud UI — REQUIRED for UI changes)
```

The matrix command creates and verifies one named bundle, then passes that
exact run to the reviewer. Do not replace the `--bundle` path with raw producer
directories; `--source` is only for explicit archived/ad-hoc compatibility.

A UI-touching diff (rendered `.tsx`/`.css`/`.svg` under `packages/app`,
`packages/ui`, `apps/app`, …) MUST attach real screenshot/video/OCR artifacts —
the CI evidence gate rejects `N/A` on those rows for such diffs, label or not.

- `shouldRunNightCheckinFromSleepCycle` — the `winding_down` immediate trigger, the 3h lead window for awake/waking owners (exact 0/180 boundaries), and the irregular-owner local-bedtime fallback (configured and 23:00 default, timezone handling, regular-owner exclusion, timezone/now requirements).
- `minutesUntilLocalBedtime` — today-vs-tomorrow roll, HH:MM validation matrix (malformed, out-of-range, whitespace), 00:00/23:59 boundaries, `Math.round` (not floor) on fractional minutes, and a UTC+9 cross-day case.
- `buildSleepRecapFromSchedule` — null schedule → null, null-baseline defaults, full-record projection.
- Constants `MORNING_CHECKIN_WINDOW_MINUTES` / `NIGHT_CHECKIN_LEAD_MINUTES` / `DEFAULT_IRREGULAR_BEDTIME_LOCAL`.

- New suite: **24/24 pass** (`bunx vitest run --config ./vitest.config.ts src/sleep/sleep-cycle-dispatch.test.ts`).

| Mutation | Result |
|---|---|
| morning window `<=` → `<` (360 boundary) | caught |
| morning guard `>= 0` semantics (zero-boundary test) | caught |
| `winding_down` trigger ignored | caught |
| night lead `<=` → `<` (180 boundary) | caught |
| regular owners get irregular fallback | caught |
| bedtime today/tomorrow roll removed | caught |
| recap `?? null` → `?? 0` zero-fill | caught |
| morning awake-state guard removed | caught |
| HH:MM hour boundary 23 → 24 | caught |

## Notable finding pinned by the suite

`shouldRunNightCheckinFromSleepCycle`'s doc block says the irregular-owner fallback applies when `minutesUntilBedtimeTarget` is null, but the implementation falls through to the fallback for **any** minutes outside `[0, 180]` — including non-null (verified by execution: `181`, `-5`, and `NaN` all still fire the fallback when the owner is near their local fallback bedtime). The suite pins the actual behavior with a test whose comment documents the JSDoc discrepancy. No production change is made in this test-only PR; flagging here for maintainer visibility.

# Risks

Low to medium — additive database authority only. This creates one seeded singleton lease row plus tenant and exact node-occurrence fairness watermarks. No caller is wired in this PR.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Adds the expand-only database foundation that later scheduler/provider slices will use to serialize backup provider effects and enforce fair service.

- Seeds one global singleton authority row for exact owner/generation/tenant/backup/operation lease identity.
- Rejects partial claims, invalid lease intervals, non-positive active claim sequences, control characters, and owner IDs outside 1–255 UTF-8 bytes.
- Adds tenant watermarks with monotone global service-sequence uniqueness.
- Keys node watermarks by immutable `source_node_history_id`, with a composite FK to the exact `(history, record, incarnation)` occurrence so an ABA reuse of record/incarnation cannot collapse two boots.
- Cascades derived fairness state with organization/live-node removal while refusing direct deletion of a referenced immutable occurrence.
- Registers idempotent migration `0313_agent_backup_operation_lane` in journal slot 296.

## What kind of change is this?

Database foundation for the backup scheduler admission flow. It is intentionally expand-only and behavior-dormant.

# Testing

Reviewer entry points:

- `packages/cloud/shared/src/db/schemas/agent-backup-operation-lane.ts`
- `packages/cloud/shared/src/db/migrations/0313_agent_backup_operation_lane.sql`
- `packages/cloud/shared/src/db/agent-backup-operation-lane-migration.pglite.test.ts`

Observed on exact head `198f3e4d34a4eb6a47b418a533d67d1b3057ba40` with Bun 1.3.14:

- real PGlite migration/invariant proof: 5/5 passed;
- global migration journal gate: 5/5 passed;
- Cloud shared TypeScript typecheck: passed;
- targeted Biome and diff-check: passed;
- `drizzle-kit check`: passed;
- independent exact-head review: CLEAN, no P0-P3.

The PGlite proof applies migration 0313 twice and exercises singleton uniqueness, partial-shape rejection, the 255-byte UTF-8 boundary, control-character rejection, tenant/node FKs, ABA-distinct node histories, counters, unique service sequences, cascade behavior, and direct history deletion fencing.

67:Te88,
## Summary

Adds `packages/core/src/messaging/interactions/index.test.ts` — a new unit suite for the message-interaction public barrel (`packages/core/src/messaging/interactions/index.ts`), which previously had no same-named test file anywhere in the repo and was not exercised by any existing suite (`interactions.test.ts` imports the submodules directly; nothing imports the barrel).

This barrel is the consumption boundary for the whole interaction engine: `index.node.ts`, `index.browser.ts`, and `index.edge.ts` each `export * from "./messaging/interactions"`, so every `@elizaos/core` consumer gets this surface through exactly this module.

## What the suite covers

All 27 cases drive the real exported runtime through `import * as interactions from "./index"` — no mocks of the subject, no type-level assertions:

- parse ↔ serialize wire round trip for choice blocks, including `allow_custom`; form field capping at `MAX_FORM_FIELDS` and unknown-type rejection; secret blocks serialize to `""`
- reply callback codec: encode/decode round trip, platform byte-budget rejection via `MAX_CALLBACK_BYTES`, foreign-payload handling
- opaque session references: deterministic entropy hexing, `is1:` prefix codec at the documented 36-byte wire size, invalid-reference and foreign-decode rejection
- response schemas derived per block kind plus validation of unknown options, unexpected fields, missing required fields, non-boolean acknowledgements, and refusal of forms carrying secret fields
- host resolution: null when absent, typed error for malformed registrations, pass-through when well-formed
- capability profiles and delivery negotiation: native preference, conversational fallback with observed limitations, sensitive-request routing, secret-field form refusal, profile-ID collision detection
- outbound text boundaries: block attachment without prose mutation, connector-safe plain-text projection, dashboard-marker stripping, button-row layout through the callback decoder

Test-only diff: **one new file, +473/-0**, no production behavior changed.
